## Kaiju v4 (horror redesign)
- flesh.js: hideTextures() paints two tiling 512px layers procedurally (scale plates: jittered-grid Voronoi, domed and shingled, dark greens, red flesh in the cracks; bare flesh: mottled dark red, bruising, veins, creases, sparse small scales) into colour, normal and roughness DataTextures; hideMaterial() is a MeshStandardMaterial patched in onBeforeCompile to blend the two layers by a vertex attribute aMask (1 scales, 0 flesh), with vertex colours for AO and horn-dark claws, and a fresnel rim (scaled by ambient light) standing in for subsurface glow. Body + Tube: one dynamic mesh of many tubes swept every frame through control points (Catmull-Rom, rings evenly spaced by arc length, frames aimed at a reference up or parallel-transported, normals from central differences, rounded closed ends, per-vertex static lumps, radius bulges per span, a radius multiplier over time).
- kaiju.js: 36 tubes in one draw call (torso with ribs, spine ridge, hunch, breathing; legs, foot pads, 3 clawed toes each; arms, 3 two-joint clawed fingers each; two necks and heads with skull, brow, sockets, hinged jaw, merged teeth rows, 3 eyes a side (the smaller head has lost one, one is milky); a drooping, lifting tail; 10 tentacles). Tentacles are verlet chains in world space pulled to a guide curve (base, out of the back, arriving at the tip target from above, writhing), length-constrained (may stretch 1.5x when busy), kept out of the torso spheres. AI: every 1.2-2.5 s a free tentacle (max 3 busy) reaches for people (grabs up to 2) or a car or slams a roof; held things ride the tip, lifted, whipped and thrown ballistically at a crowd, or dropped if interrupted / on retreat. Heads look about independently, snap every ~8 s, roar 0.3 s apart with an inhale. The body is only re-swept when its bounding sphere is in the frustum. Costs: ~140 ms for the textures at first spawn, ~0.7 ms per frame for tentacles + sweep on desktop. Be mode: tap people to send a tentacle; label counts grabbed. Roster tile animates (userData.tick), fit 62.
- Heroes vs the monsters (heroes.assign, job monster): Tempo casts a tracked 6 s bubble on the kaiju (R 42) or the breaching worm (R 30) every 9 s - D.slowAt now scales Kaiju.update, its tentacle sim and Worm.update; Dragonfly flies to a tentacle holding people, kaiju.rescue(q) drops any car softly and hands the people over (job ferry: carried 70 m away, people.setDown puts them on the street unhurt, fleeing); Nova and Dragonfly punch a kaiju head (8.4 / 4.2) or the worm head while it is above ground (12 / 6), knocked back after each hit. Kaiju hp 1200 (life 300), worm hp 900 (life 240; at 0 it goes deep). Auto responders light the hero signal for the worm and kaiju too. Typical: kaiju driven off in ~95 s (with airstrikes), worm in ~155 s / 6 breaches.
- Follow camera director (game.direct/shot): the subject's heading is smoothed from its motion (camDir); shot(s) gives distance, polar angle and a side (base +/- off, the nearer of the two) per situation - kaiju walk (low 3/4 front, 330), act (290), roar (low front, 260), leaving (from behind); worm digging (from behind and above) or leaping (side on, centred on the middle of the arc, turns fast: rate 2.2); a fight within 50 m (side on to the pair, 140+); Blur dashing (from behind), Tyrant holding something (wider), heroes on a monster (both in view). Re-aimed when the situation key changes or every 8 s; eases at 0.8/s (azimuth, polar) and 1.2/s (distance); distance x up to 1.8 for portrait screens. controls.minDistance is now 120 (was 200).
- Day and night: main.js used to place the sun light along a fixed sunDir, so shadows never moved; it now uses daylight.sunDir. Daylight.aim(arc, elev): the sun crosses from 75 deg one side of azNoon (az0 - 0.49) to 75 deg the other (sunArc: rises at phase 0.9, highest at 0, sets at 0.4), elevation floor 3 deg for long low shadows, intensity 5 x sunK with no floor; the moon (moonElevation: up 0.42..0.87, 52 deg at midnight, same arc) takes over the one directional light once the sun is gone (intensity 1.6 x moonK x night), so the hand-overs at dusk and dawn happen at zero light.
- Throat drag (brawl.drag/dragStep): picks the way (of 8, 80 m, inside the city) with the most cars and small buildings to plough; the holder flies 2.2 m up at up to 34 m/s for 2.4 s with the victim (gripped) 3 m behind on the ground; every 2.2 m a scour gouge (aligned, rot = atan2(-dz, dx)) and cracks, cars within 7 m flung sideways and on, parked cars burnt, people pushed aside and panicked; buildings entered and left are smashed (smash: windows, debris, small ones collapse); ends with a toss onwards (22 m/s) and hurt 35. Heroes: 30% grab-and-throw, 20% drag; Tyrant brawl plan: 45% drag.
- Sandbox has two follow buttons: Bad guys (game.cycleFollow: villains, kaiju, worm, riot, tripods, saucers, then zombies) and Good guys (game.cycleGood: super heroes, helicopters, mission vehicles driving - named by kind - and the 10 responders on foot nearest the view: police officers, soldiers, firefighters, crews (FIREFIGHTER kind in HELD)). subjects(side), followSubject(s, side); the active button shows the name (green / blue). Shots: vehicles from behind and high (pol 0.62), people on foot close (150) looking down into the street.
- Collision: tripods keep 42 m apart (Invasion.spread r 21 each, firmer push between two tripods; STANCE is 17) and are lowered at least 50 m from the others. Tyrant carrying a building: lifted straight up out of its plot before it swings across (ky over t 0-0.55, kx over 0.35-1); flyY also clears the roofs 1.5 s ahead; and if the carried shell still overlaps another building it smashes into it once (smash) and he is heaved up over it (H.scraped). Measured: 0 frames of a carried building inside another over 4 carries.

## Performance plan (Sean chose: stars only, texture detail, detail up close, crowds and cars simple)
- Black squares on the kaiju: one NaN pixel blurred into the glow mip chain (tBlur lod 4/6) spread to ~128 px blocks. post.js clean() clamps every pixel read with min(max(c,0),64000) (isnan() gets compiled away by ANGLE/D3D); Tube.update skips non-finite control points, borrows the previous tangent for coincident rings and never writes a zero normal; a tentacle whose tip goes non-finite is laid out afresh.
- Follow lock: while following, controls pan off, one finger / left mouse rotates (lockControls), pinch zooms; touching sets manualCam so the director stops re-aiming; the Stop button in the follow chip (zd-stop) ends the follow.
- searchlights.js: up to 18 additive cone beams + ground pools, planned 4x a second: helicopters on the nearest target (or sweeping below), the tallest roof 45-170 m from each of up to 4 targets, the 6 mission vehicles nearest the view within 140 m of a target. Targets: game.subjects(bad) positions plus other disasters' poi. Fade with daylight.night.
- Stars: villains.js smoothBody/detail - heroes and villains get a tube body (torso with belt and emblem, head with hair, legs into boots, arms into gloves; vertex-coloured) built when first within 330 m of the camera, swept each sync; the boxy parts hide (eyes and ponytail kept). Roster figures always smooth. Tripods: within 400 m the 3 legs and 6 tentacles are tubes through the same joints (Body, world space) instead of 90 instanced cylinders. Worm: hideMaterial with the SANDHIDE palette (pale, dry) under its vertex-coloured bands, aMask from the belly, scales 4x around; its head in the same hide (mask 1, no vertex colours, repeat 3). flesh.js hideTextures(palette) is cached per palette; hideMaterial options mask, vertexColors, color, repeat.
- Buildings: textures.js relief() makes a normal map from each painted canvas (luminance as height: dark sits back, light stands out) for the four facades, the shop strip and the roof; city.js uses them (normalScale 1.6 walls, 1.5 roofs) and walls() darkens walls towards the street (0.74 at the kerb, full by 6 m, from cutGround's vHoleW). Kaiju: beyond 560 m its body is re-swept every other frame. Busy night scene at phone settings: 3.6M triangles, 122 draw calls (empty city 3.9M, 80).
- Catching Blur: a hero can get hold of him while a time bubble holds him (and he has not just broken free) or while he is dazed after landing from a throw (Blur.landed: dazed 1.4 s, stuck and freeT reset). Hero.catchBlur dives in at 90 m/s (heroes are not slowed by bubbles) and at 5 m (gcd 1.5 s) grabs and throws him through a building (50%), drags him down the street (30%) or punches him flat. Tempo (doSlow) catches within 120 m after bubbling; Nova and Dragonfly get a 'blur' job (doBlur: shadow him, catch within 220 m) once their own villain is beaten or absent, and go back to their rival if it returns. Blur.frail = 2 (brawl.hurt doubles hits on him). Tested: Blur beaten 60-65 s into a three-villain fight (about 12 catches, all by Tempo); Blur alone, Nova and Dragonfly do the catching (beaten at 59 s).
- Tsunami rescue (Sean: send more than one heli or boat at a time, help people drowning all over the city, heroes helping, options that do nothing, an inflatable barrier):
  - People afloat: new state ST.SWIM 29 (in INHASH, HITTABLE, GRABBABLE; not CIV, so crowds, zombies and sirens ignore them; anim 12 = arms up waving, in figures.js). people.swim(i, secs): floats at the water surface (y = level - 1.3), drifts off walls, drowns when t1 runs out, stands up when the water is under 0.9 m. fx 4 swimming to a raft (px, pz), fx 1 holding on (120 s, not tiring). Wading in deep water 5 s turns a civilian into a swimmer (was: drown at 16 s). Landing from a throw in deep water: 75% come up swimming (35-80 s). The tsunami tumbles people back into the water behind it (fling 6-12 m/s, was 15-22, which landed them on dry ground ahead) and W.wetLag 1 m (gameplay water starts right behind the front; the drawn surface still lags 9 m). people.swimSpots(n), nearestSwimmer(x, z, R), toRoof(i, k), roofPerson(k), hoist(j), winched(j) (not 'lifted': that name is an array on People), alarmAt(x, z, R). stats.afloat.
  - Rescue helicopters (aircraft.js hoistStep): hover 11 m over the roof or 15 m over the water, lower a rope (0.6 s), winch one survivor up (1.3 s, EXT with fx 3 = holding the rope), then the next; k = -1 works over water and edges across to swimmers within 110 m. 12 a trip, 45 s max. Water helicopters with opts.onDrop drop life rafts instead of water.
  - Boats: once there they motor across open water to swimmers within 90 m (b.goal), stay up to 70 s. Boats.dropRafts(x, z): 4 rafts (orange ring, yellow canopy), each draws the 6 nearest swimmers. Sandbox auto boats every 4 s up to 8 when more than 40 are afloat.
  - Sandbox responders: up to 4 rescue helicopters over swimmer spots (every 6 s), life rafts every 16 s when more than 25 are afloat; the hero signal for a tsunami (warn or wave) and for a flood with more than 20 afloat.
  - Heroes: 'swim' job (everyone free when anyone is afloat): two at a time, carried to the nearest standing roof 3 m clear of the water (toRoof). Tempo's 'wave' job: a 46 m bubble on the front nearest the view (cd 13); SweepFront.advance multiplies by D.slowAt. letGo() drops carried people back in the water when the heroes leave.
  - Commander: FLOOD_CD boat 5 s, rescue 8 s, water 15 s once flooded (cooldowns cut when the flood starts, with a toast). Ground units (police, bus, fire, apc, chaser, build, camp, hazmat) are refused and greyed while flooded. New cards: Coast Guard (250, cd 60, unlock 3: no tap, 3 boats and 2 helicopters to the busiest swimmer spots) and Flood barrier (200, cd 25, unlock 5: only while a tsunami warns or hits). Rescue heli on swimmers (within 45 m of the tap) winches from the water; water heli over a flood drops rafts; police during a tsunami warning send everyone within 90 m up to the roofs.
  - Barriers (barrier.js, D.barriers): 64 m orange tube, R 2.6, inflates in 3 s, across the wave (along its tangent), snapped to a street. SweepFront holds sections whose tangent coordinate is within the barrier at b.stop (5 m in front, so the lip does not hide it), pile 1.15, and skips the slope limit for held sections so the rest of the wave goes round. After 22 s of contact it gives way: sw.tameRange marks those sections tame (0.6 speed, no flinging, no building damage).
  - Tested (sandbox, auto responders): up to ~900 afloat; rescued 207 (was 105) and drowned 716 (was 901, before which nobody survived the wave to be in the water). Commander with all cards: no errors, barrier held 22 s.
- Bus boarding (Sean: see them get on): people.busDoor(busId) = the front door on the side with room (cached in people.doors per stop, cleared by releaseBus and reset). BOARD runs to the door; within 2.6 m they wait their turn (watch 1, idle), then one every 0.42 s walks to the door and up the steps into the bus (watch 2, 0.9 s, y +0.55) before EVAC. Police buses too (custodyToBus).
- Zombie hordes (Sean: travel in hordes like the riot, trap people, drawn to fleeing cars until something new turns their heads): people.hordeTick (1 Hz): hord[i] = horde id (Map hordes); 4+ zombies within 22 m band together, stragglers within 45 m join, members over 90 m away drop out, under 3 and it breaks up. hordeAim: a car (score 0.1 if panicking or burning, else 0.025, x speed / distance) or a crowd within 60 m (min(n, 20) x 0.1 / distance); a new focus has to beat the current one by 1.5x. Car: goal ahead of it. People: a ring round them that closes in (13 m - 1.6 m a second). Nothing: the horde drifts down the streets. hordeStep: each member keeps its own place in the mass (golden-angle slots), walking at chase speed when it has a focus; prey close by still triggers the normal chase. Alerted zombies (gunshots, screamers, Be-mode lure) ignore the horde for a while. Infection rate about the same as before (two runs each, wide spread); 1.6-1.9 ms a step.
- Dust storm look (Sean's video and photo: the Fury Road storm, a wave of dirt with lightning and twisters): fronts.js DustWall replaces the puffs and curtains. One sheet (34 rows along the cross-section x 240 along the front, built only across the city plus 320 m) bent to PROF: a rolling nose at the foot, the face leaning back, a curl 0.2 H forward over it near the top, the body 0.8 H behind; H 280 with taller and lower stretches. Vertex: push() = tall billows climbing (fbm, 80 m), small ones on them, ropes of dust twisting down the face every 110 m (65% of slots), lumpy dust balls rolling at the toe; the normal is tilted by the slope of push() (finite differences) so billows catch the light. Fragment: sun + sky + a little view light, dark folds, darker top, paler low dust, lightning glow (uFlash, 70 m falloff). DustLightning: every 1.4-5.2 s near the view, 60% a forked bolt (core + additive halo cylinders, HDR) from under the curl to the street ahead (a spark burst there), 40% a glow inside the wall only; 0.32 s, flickering. DustDevils (7, low 4): snaking open cones 50-140 m tall following 30-230 m behind the front, throwing street furniture up, scattering people (4% lifted). BillowWall is no longer used by the haboob (layers/small options added). 2.4 ms a step, 60 fps on the desktop.
- Sound effects (audio.js; Sean: explosions with bass, crashes and breaking glass as real as possible, cheap on fps): everything synthesized in JS at 24 kHz (noise, RBJ biquads, two-pole resonator 'ping', falling-sine 'thump', tanh saturation), rendered into AudioBuffers in 12 ms chunks after the first tap (all of it ~0.9 s), variations per sound. play(name, x, y, z, {gain, rate, delay, reach}) = one BufferSource -> lowpass (muffled with distance) -> gain (reach / distance) -> StereoPanner (screen x) -> bus -> compressor -> master; GAP (least time between), CAP (most at once per sound), MAXV 22. Sounds: boom (car, grenade), boomBig (airstrike, eruption, saucer and tripod down), nuke, crash (dull hit, 22 damped panel modes struck 14 times, crumple grains, a window, a scrape), glass / glassBig (crack, 55/170 ringing shards 2-10 kHz, burst, pieces landing), collapse, shot / rifle (with street echoes), thunder, whoomph (fire catching), splash / splashBig, zap, thud. The explosions' sub thump is overdriven so its harmonics carry the bass on phone speakers. Events mapped in Audio.event (game.update drains every event list through it); direct sfx() calls in blowWindows, the dust storm's lightning and brawl.hurt (blows landing); people emit 'boom' for grenades, the tsunami's 'tsunamihit' now carries x, z. Loops (seamless crossfaded buffers): city hum, wind (tornado, dust storm, cold wave, dust and sand weather), water (tsunami, floods), fire (fires near the view), rain, rotor (nearest helicopter, panned); a siren is a live sawtooth with a 0.23 Hz wail on the nearest rescue unit driving. Sound button (top 42% of the right edge) saved in localStorage zday_sound; navigator.audioSession 'playback' so it plays with the iPhone's silent switch on (Safari 16.4+); suspended while the tab is hidden. 60 fps with 13 voices at once on the desktop.
- Follow the disaster (Sean: Bad guys should always track the disaster; a following angle for tornadoes, a leading angle for the dust and solar storms): game.subjects('bad') now adds every other live disaster (HAZARDS names, plus 'Nuclear blast') after the villains, monsters, riots and aliens and before the zombies. game.front(e) = the point of a storm front (tsunami, haboob, coldwave via e.sw; solar via origin, dir, ws) level with the view, its heading and whether it has passed (tsunami warning, recede, after). disasterAt(e): 45 m ahead of a front at y 12, the middle of a tornado's funnel, a falling meteor or missile (e.ball, e.missile), the busiest spot of people afloat in a flood, else the disaster's own spot (volcano at y 40). disasterShot(e): fronts from ahead looking back (base = heading, off 0.4, dist 560, dust storm 700, pol 0.94, rate 1.4); tornado chased from behind and low (heading + PI, off 0.55, dist 480, pol 1.0); meteor and missile from low (pol 1.02); sinkhole looking down into it; quake, volcano, flood and the rest standing back.
- Sound round 2 (Sean: sirens too loud and carrying too far; sounds for villain lasers and the like; crashes sound off; wind and rain too loud, like static): the siren is 0.09 at most and gone by 340 m from the camera. crash() and collapse() rebuilt from noise (no ringing tones): a slap and a heavy low body, crumpling as noise chopped into jagged 2-8 ms bursts, the windscreen, trim clattering down; a collapse is a crack, then six floors pancaking (thump + low-passed burst each) under a swelling roar, rubble grains, glass, grit trickling. New: laser (Tyrant's eyes and car zaps, tripod heat rays lower), web (Weaver), whoosh (Blur starting a dash), warp (Tempo's bubbles), roar / roarDeep (the kaiju's two heads, the worm surfacing), horn (a tripod landing and every 22-40 s while it walks), thud on hero punches. Wind and rain loops softer (more low-passed) and quieter (wind 0.12-0.55, rain 0.18, storm 0.3). DUCK: the music dips under nukes, airstrikes, collapses and roars.
- Music (music.js): public/music/calm.mp3, storm.mp3, rebuild.mp3 (Sean's Suno tracks Calm Before the Storm, Desert Pursuit, After the Storm; 128 kbps, cover art stripped, ~3 MB each). Two media-element players taking turns through createMediaElementSource (streamed, never decoded whole): mood storm while any disaster is alive (not heroes) or more than 12 zombies, rebuild for 150 s after, calm otherwise; 4 s crossfades; 6 s before a track ends it crossfades back into its own start. prime() on the first taps starts both players from a gesture (phones need that). Its own output straight to the destination, so the sound and music buttons are independent. Both buttons step loud, medium, quiet, off (sound 0.85 / 0.55 / 0.3, music 0.62 / 0.38 / 0.2), drawn by levelIcon (icon over three bars), saved as zday_sound / zday_music 0-3.
- Calling the heroes (Sean: in Sandbox, let disasters hit unless I call for help; heroes should win only 2 times in 3): Responders.autoHeroes false, so nothing calls them automatically. Sandbox.threat() finds villains, the kaiju or the worm, a meteor or missile on its way, a tsunami; while there is one and no heroes are here, a flashing Call heroes button shows (and a toast). callHeroes() lights the signal over the threat. heroesWin(o) in heroes.js rolls once per villain, monster, meteor or missile (ODDS 0.66): a lost meteor or missile knocks the hero flying (Hero.lost) and it comes down; a villain shrugs off the last blow (hp back to 390) and the heroes fall back (Villains.beat); the kaiju and the worm swat them away at the last blow (damage). heroLost on anything means no hero goes after it again. Tested: 11 of 15 meteors saved; forced losses against all three villains.
- Round of fixes (Sean): helicopter searchlights start under the nose (hl.hx/hz * 6, y + 1.4). The follow camera moves on to another of the same side (good or bad) that is inside the city, or stops, once what it follows leaves the city (game.followIn: it has to have been inside first). Parked cars no longer overlap traffic: LANE 2.5 -> 2.15 and the kerb-side parked cars 4.1 -> 4.35 from the road centre (2.2 m centre to centre alongside, was 1.6; 0 overlaps in 6550 samples); a sliding wreck stops against a parked car and dents it (darkened, a crash sound). Solar flare: as the wave passes each driving car, 40% lose control (traffic.crash, skidding into others), 20% stall, the rest panic; sparking lamps (12%) and blowing transformers near the view (16%) start fires through Solar.spark (a building nearby or a parked car, 24 at most). Tested: 176 crashes and 17 building fires in 25 s. Water jets: D.jets (a Splash, droplets in 'v' exact-velocity mode) via fire.jet(x0, y0, z0, x1, y1, z1, n, size), thrown on the ballistic arc that lands on the target; firefighters on a hose throw a jet at the wall (2 a step, size 0.9); fire trucks parked at a fire (D.cannons) arc a roof-cannon jet onto the burning building within 55 m (4 a step, size 1.6) and douse it at 0.6x.
- The world (Sean: a globe with the big city and smaller towns, zoom out to see the world, no engine rewrite). Plan: 1 the curve, 2 a town builder, 3 a globe view (spin it, tap a place, fly in), 4 a connected world (places remember damage, meteors and storms on the globe).
  - 1 The curve (curve.js): every material in the scene gets its vertex shader bent as it compiles (curveFrame traverses the scene each frame, wraps new materials' onBeforeCompile once, custom cache key): built-ins add, after project_vertex, the world position's drop k * d^2 from the view's centre (works in the shadow pass too: MeshDepthMaterial / MeshDistanceMaterial prototypes bend as well); our own ShaderMaterials go back from gl_Position to world with inverse(P * V) and drop the same way; full-screen passes are left alone. k = K_MAX 2.2e-4 x smoothstep over camera distance 300-2200 (none close in). curvedHit traces taps onto the bent ground (game.worldPoint). maxDistance 2200 (was 1100), camera far 7000, shadow camera grows with the zoom (300-950 m), the haze thins zoomed out (weather.apply x min(1, 1000 / dist)). ?curve=0 turns it off.
  - 2 Towns (places.js): PLACES metro (the big city), harbor (Harbor Town: 7 blocks, white and pastel, a domed town hall, up to 7 floors), dust (Dust Creek: 6 blocks, sandstone, up to 4 floors), pines (Pine Hollow: 5 blocks, brick, up to 5 floors). buildCity opts: blocks, seed, maxFloors, tall (share of 7-12 storey buildings), landmarks, centers, diag, tint (extra wall material weights). People and cars scale by place.people / place.cars. ?place=key, else the last place (localStorage zday_place); travel(key, mode) rebuilds the page there. Travel on the main menu (a list with blurbs) and a Travel row in the Sandbox panel.
  - 3 The globe (globe.js): the 🌍 button (right edge, under the sound and music buttons) opens it; built the first time (about 0.2 s on the desktop): a planet of R 10 painted from 3D value noise on the sphere (no seams): deep and shallow sea paling into beaches, grass, forest (moisture), desert (dry, under 34 degrees), rock and snow on the heights, ice caps over 68 degrees; a cloud sphere drifting, an additive atmosphere rim, stars. 1024x512 map (768x384 on small screens). The places are put where they suit (city: deep in a big landmass that reaches the sea; harbour: solid land at the water's edge; desert; forest), apart but on one side, the same every time (seeded); each is an InstancedMesh cluster of little buildings in its colours; a pulsing ring marks where you are; DOM labels follow them on the facing side. OrbitControls (no pan, 1.25-5 R). Opening pulls the camera back off your place (1.6 s); tapping a label flies down over it (1.4 s) and travel()s there (your own place just closes the globe). While it is open main.js skips the city frame (game paused, city controls off), the HUD is hidden and game taps are ignored. toVec uses SphereGeometry's wrap (z = -cos(lat) sin(lon)).
  - 4 A connected world (world.js, localStorage zday_world): places remember the buildings that came down (Sandbox only: saved every 5 s, on pagehide and when the globe opens; replayed on load as hideBuilding + addRubble, people sent out first; Reset or rebuilding clears them); the globe smokes over places with 3+ down and shows craters (dark discs, glowing for a minute when fresh; the last 20 kept). The globe's ☄ Send a meteor button arms a tap on the planet: a burning rock streaks in from deep space (1.7 s, a trail), a flash and a shockwave ring, a crater; within 0.1 rad of a place it is a direct hit: sendMeteor(place, offset in metres, size 34) and the camera flies down 2.2 s later; on arrival (load, or closing the globe over your own place) takeMeteor starts a meteor there (warn 7 s). Tested in the built-in browser: a meteor sent onto Pine Hollow flew down and came in at its centre; a size-34 meteor in Harbor Town took 70 buildings, no hang (the Playwright MCP browser froze during that test and had to be abandoned). Game toasts are hidden while the globe is open.
  - More places: bayview (Bayview, a second city: 10 blocks, up to 14 floors, Art Deco tower and stadium, glass and concrete), lakeside (Lakeside, 6 blocks, pastel and brick, a dome), mesa (Red Mesa, a 4-block red adobe hamlet). place.land (desert, forest, coast) leans the outskirts that way (outskirts LAND weights on east desert, south beach, north mountains, west forest): Dust Creek and Red Mesa sit in desert all round, Pine Hollow and Lakeside in forest and hills, Harbor Town and Bayview by the sea; Metro City keeps its mixed ring. The globe places all seven (WANTS city / coast / desert / forest).
- UI clean-up (Sean: card bar items off the right edge could not be reached; make the UI and menus symmetrical and intuitive; on a Samsung in Chrome the Disasters panel ran up under the address bar; Good guys / Bad guys should say what they do):
  - ui.js: sideButton(id, title) puts the sound, music and globe buttons in one column (#zd-side, right edge, vertically centred; hidden while the Sandbox panel is open via body.zd-panel; a row at the top right while the menu is open, body.zd-menu-on). dragScroll(el, axis) scrolls rows and panels by hand from pointer events (touch-action none on the element, glide after a flick, a drag is never a click, mouse wheel turns into sideways scroll on rows) so it is the same on every phone whatever the body's touch-action:none does; returns by(d) to glide.
  - The bar (#zd-bar) is a column: .zd-extra (things that come and go: Call the heroes, the follow chip), .zd-cardwrap (Commander cards), .zd-dock (equal-width buttons, a line icon over a short name; ICON by button id). game.addButton puts buttons in the dock (EXTRA ids above it, zd-card buttons in their row); setLabel(b, text) changes a dock button's name without losing its icon. --zd-bar-h (ResizeObserver) places the fps text and the panel above it. The HUD pills are centred.
  - Sandbox dock: Disasters (orange, it is the main thing) | Follow disaster | Follow rescue | speed | Reset | Menu. While following, the pressed button reads Next disaster / Next rescue and a chip above the bar says Following <name> with a Stop button (replaces the X button). The Disasters button reads Cancel or Done (n left) while something waits to be placed.
  - Sandbox panel: tabs Disasters (with the Help row) | Weather | Travel; flex rows of three with a short last row centred; max-height = the space between the bar and the bottom of the HUD (fitPanel on open and resize), the body scrolls (dragScroll).
  - Commander cards: g.addCardRow(): the row scrolls by finger (dragScroll), fades at an edge with more beyond, and shows round arrows there that glide 70% of the width.
  - End screens (Commander, Be): game.endScreen(el, head, lines, buttons) puts the result and the buttons at the top and the figures under them; the box scrolls (dragScroll) and sits above the side buttons (z 35). Only the main button is green (.go: On to <town>, else Play again).
- Menu over the globe, and the road of towns (Sean: the globe as the menu's backdrop, turning slowly; small menu buttons; Commander and Be The Disaster go where you left off, the smallest town the first time, working up to the big city; Sandbox: the menu drops away and you pick the city):
  - places.js PATH = mesa, pines, dust, lakeside, harbor, bayview, metro (smallest first). travel(key, mode, extra) (extra: more URL settings, e.g. kind for Be).
  - profile.js: data.path[mode] = how far the road is open (index), data.last[mode] = the town played last; reach(mode), town(mode) (last played if open, else the furthest), played(mode, key) (Commander and Be start()), clear(mode, key) (a win in the furthest open town opens the next; returns its key once).
  - A win: Commander 2+ stars; Be The Disaster a score of 30 per building in the town (Red Mesa 195 buildings: 5,850). The end screen says New town open / what it takes, and On to <town> (game.journey: the globe rises off this town, then flies down to the next one, globe.after).
  - globe.js states: game (opened from a place: the 🌍 button), menu (backdrop(): turning slowly, autoRotate 0.45, the whole planet fitted across the screen (fit()), the view offset up (setViewOffset, shift 0.14 upright) so it sits above the menu card, labels small and under the card (z 5), the road shown), pick (choose({title, sub, arrive, back}): Sandbox, tap any place), path (choose({key, road, status, arrive}): turn to the town 1.3 s, then fly down). arrive(key): the same place closes the globe and starts the mode here, anywhere else travel()s. status(key) (game.townStatus(mode)) labels the road: '2 · Pine Hollow', a tick for won towns, a lock (dimmed, refuses with why) for towns not open; also used when the 🌍 button is opened in Commander and Be (globe.statusFor). The road: a thin gold tube along great circles from town to town (buildRoad).
  - game.js menu: the title over the planet, a card at the bottom: level and XP bar, three tiles (Commander: 'Level n · town', Be the Disaster: the same, Sandbox: 'Pick any city'), a row with Units, Roster and the sound and music buttons (game.dockSide moves #zd-side into .mside; main.js calls it again once they exist), the totals. playMode(which, kind): the card drops away (.gone), then globe.choose. Be the Disaster opens its list first; Play there goes to its town. The menu's Travel button is gone (Sandbox picks on the globe). game.modeName = menu / commander / be / sandbox.
  - While the globe is up the 🌍 button hides and sound and music sit in the top right (body.zd-globe-on).
- Through the clouds (Sean: a smoother trip to the globe, full zoom out of the city and through clouds, as if leaving the world): sky.js Sky. A DOM cloud layer (#zd-sky, z 40: a pale fill and three tiling puff layers from a 192 px canvas, each growing and fading as it passes; cover 0-1, to(k, secs), moving speeds them up). up(then): the city camera pulls up to 2600 m and nearly straight down (polar 0.22) in 1.5 s (ease in), the clouds close from 42% of the way; then globe.open({clouds}) starts at R 1.07 over the place and rises 2.1 s to fit() * 0.8 while the clouds clear (1 s). Down: globe.go flies to R 1.05 in 1.6 s and the clouds close from 58%; the same place: globe.close() calls sky.down() (from 2600 m down to the view it left, 2.3 s, ease out, clouds clear in 1.1 s); another place: Sky.leaving() (sessionStorage zd_sky) then travel; index.html turns the page pale while it loads (html.sky) and main.js starts inside the clouds and descends (300 ms after load; to game.zoomTo if a mode set one). While travelling the controls are loosened (minPolar 0, maxDistance 3000) and off; main.js calls sky.update every frame and sky.step after game.update. globe.up(after) (main.js) = Follow off, sky.up, globe.open({clouds}), then on to after; the 🌍 button, game.journey (On to <town>) and the Sandbox panel's Travel tab all go this way. The globe's buttons hide while it dives; labels only while it is up; the ring round your place fades in as the clouds clear.
- Compass (Sean: so people know which way is which; the districts are named North-East and so on): #zd-compass on the left edge (mirrors the sound and music column), a card with a red N needle and E S W turned so N points to -z as the camera sees it (game.compassStep, rotate = atan2(off.x, off.z)); a tap turns the view to face north (0.6 s). Hidden in the menu, while the Sandbox panel is open and on the globe.
- Roster in the game (Sean: like the other menu screens, floating over the earth): showcase.js became roster.js startRoster({ canvas, list, root }) -> { stop }; the menu's Roster opens a tall card (no backdrop blur, so the canvas, z 2 inside #zd-menu, sits between the card and the labels) and loads roster.js on demand; stopRoster frees the WebGL context when the panel changes or the menu goes. showcase.html still works on its own (src/showcase.js calls startRoster). The globe's names hide behind the menu's other panels (backdrop(names)).
- Town outlines and more towns (Sean: less square cities, different shapes, more of them; the globe's little towns should look like the cities): shapes.js townMask(shape, NB, seed) -> (i, j) built or open land; shapes ragged, round, diamond, cross, ell (north-east quarter open), crescent (a bay to the east), long (a strip along x), twin (two neighbourhoods and the road between); the edge is jittered by a hash of (i, j, seed), the middle block(s) always built. city.js: land(i, j) blocks get landBlock (grass, woods for forest land, scrub for desert, from their own rng rl so the town's dice are not shifted), no walk block, no superblocks or landmarks there; downtown centres are drawn in until they are on built ground; roads with land both sides have no parked cars or lamps and few street trees; no crossings among fields. The street grid itself is unchanged (traffic and people untouched). world.js v2 forgets remembered damage (building numbers moved).
  - Places now 11 (PATH order): mesa (round, 5), pines (ell, 6), crossing Cross Plains (cross, 7, new), dust (diamond, 7), lakeside (crescent, 7), harbor (long, 9), sunvale Sun Valley (round desert, 8, dome, new), ironside Ironside (ell mill town, 9, new), northgate Northgate (diamond, 10, deco tower, new), bayview (twin, 11, two downtowns), metro (ragged).
  - Globe models: per place, its grid on the tangent plane (east, south) at the spot, 0.045 globe units a block: asphalt pads and pale pavements under the built blocks, four buildings a block coloured from the place's wall materials (palette: city.js MATERIALS weighted like the builder plus the tint), cities (over 8 floors) taller round seeded downtown centres, towns low and even; domes, stadium rings and towers. The ring round your place is sized to it. WANTS has 'grass' (Cross Plains, Ironside).
- Disaster Radio (Sean's ElevenLabs announcer, "Max - 1940s RP British voice", script in VOICEOVER.md): the 130 s recording had no long gaps between lines, so it was cut by lining the script up with its pauses (expected length from syllables and punctuation, dynamic programming over the pauses under -40 dB; checked: the loudest pieces are the shouting lines, the quietest the calm, whispered and sighing ones) into public/radio/01.mp3..24.mp3 (32 kHz mono 64 kbps, faded ends, 1.1 MB). radio.js Radio: lines fetched and decoded the first time wanted; played through highpass 340 / peaking 1900 +5 dB / lowpass 3600 / a tanh crunch into the effects bus (the sound button's volume), a looped static hiss with a crackle at either end, the music held down (music.hold(k, secs)); the words in #zd-radio just above the bar (italic, KDIS tag; hidden on the globe, in the menu and while the Sandbox panel is open; shown even with the sound off, timed for reading). When: each new disaster in D.list (FOR by type; Be The Disaster's kinds too: lava, saucer, tripod), zombies appearing, the heroes arriving (21), all clear 7 s after the last disaster ends (22, 23, 24 in turn), the call sign 3 s after a game starts (radio.begin; Be announces its disaster instead). No line again within 45 s; two waiting at most (the latest news). The game's own clock (paused with the game).
- Glass (Sean: too much and too often, especially in riots): a riot's panicking traffic crashes a lot and every crash had a windscreen in it. Now one crash sound in three has a windscreen (at 0.28); glass 0.32 / glassBig 0.4 volume, at least 0.9 / 1.4 s apart, one at a time; crashes 0.6 volume, 0.3 s apart, two at once.
- Commander round of changes (Sean: a pause button; not the big red circles: flash the disaster a few blips, a small Show button that takes the camera there and marks it with a scope that shrinks to the spot; send several units while the points last, grey only when you cannot afford it):
  - Pause: #zd-pause on the left over the compass (all modes, not the menu or with the Sandbox panel open); game.setPaused: run = 0 stops the simulation, the round clock (mode.update gets sdt 0), realT, helicopters, disasters, fire and the radio; the camera, follow and taps still work; PAUSED shown mid-screen. Startmode unpauses.
  - Incidents: Commander.report(x, z, text, radius, exact, name, mark) no longer toasts or shows the #zd-call strip (gone) or the ground rings (pins gone). game.alertFlash(name, hint, show): the name in a red pill blinking 4 times under the HUD with the rest of the text small under it (3.2 s), and #zd-alertchip in the bar's extra row (dot, name, Show; hides after 45 s). Names: threatName(hazard) for warn events, Zombies / Zombie sighting for 911 calls, Tornado / Tripod / Saucer from the storm chasers, Airstrike and Nuke (mark: the scope straight away, the camera left alone).
  - The scope (Commander.scope, aimAt, scopeStep on the real clock): red outer ring, inner ring, centre dot and four ticks flat on the ground (depthTest off), 4.5x wide and turning, closing to 0.6 of the search radius in 1.1 s, pulsing, gone by 7.2 s.
  - Units: no cooldowns except the nuke (once a round); spend() keeps the card picked while the points cover another, so each tap on the map sends one more; cards grey only for points, alert level, the nuke used, or what the flood allows.
  - Globe labels: where two would overlap, the one nearer the middle of the view shows (your place and the town being flown to first); widths cached per label (d._w, reset by relabel).
- Fire trucks from level 1 (Sean: level 1 needs fire rescue for the fires: riots and outbreaks start them): UNLOCK.fire 1, the card needs no alert level, and it sits next to Police.
- Radio fix (Sean heard nothing, only saw the words): the first cut put -ss after -i, so afade=out ran on the recording's own clock and every line after the first was silent. Re-cut with -ss before -i, each line with up to 0.16 s before it and 0.22 s after (shared out of the pause), 15 ms fade in, 80 ms fade out; all 24 checked loud in the browser (-17 to -25 dB). Check loudness, not just that a file decodes.
- Lasers (Sean: too high pitched, keep the lows): laser() is now an 88 Hz saw low-passed at 650 Hz, a 44 Hz throb and a brown-noise crackle, all low-passed at 1.4 kHz (no 2.35 kHz whine, no 3 kHz sizzle); zap() (the aliens' ray guns) falls 770 to 120 Hz, low-passed at 1.5 kHz.
- Follow fix (Sean: following a helicopter showed only water): a helicopter heading home (state out) stayed on the list until 300 m past the edge, and followIn only let go of a subject that had been inside the city; the view is clamped to the city, so it sat at the edge over the sea. Now a helicopter flying off counts as gone (next rescuer), a subject outside the city for 2.5 s is let go either way (outT), and the follow buttons only pick what is in the city or within 40 m of it (game.inCity(q, m)).
- Sound after the trip (Sean's screen recording: the sound went at the fly-down and never came back): going to another town rebuilds the page, and a new page cannot make a sound (Web Audio or the music) until it is tapped again (iPhone and Chrome autoplay rules; nothing carries across a page load). Arriving through the clouds now shows a small 'Tap anywhere for sound' note (audio.askTap; gone on the first tap, which starts the sound and the music); the radio holds its call sign until the sound is on (or 15 s). The town label no longer shows over the clouds while the page goes (globe.leaving). Building the next town without a page load would remove the tap but means rebuilding the whole world in place.
- Radio round 2 (Sean: lower, more compressed and distorted, its own volume button): highpass 420 / peaking 1800 +7 dB / lowpass 3200, driven x2.6 into a harder lopsided tanh (k 4, offset 0.18, 2x oversampled), then its own DynamicsCompressor (-32 dB, 14:1, 2 ms) and its own volume (radio.out to the destination, not the effects bus): LEVELS 0 / 0.55 / 1 / 1.6, starting at medium (measured on line 3: medium -16 dB rms, 5.5 dB under the old radio; loud -12). The hiss goes through the same compressor. The 📻 button (#zd-radiob, third in the side column, in the menu card too) steps loud, medium, quiet, off (zday_radio); off means no voice and no words. audio.radioOn keeps the sound running when only the radio is on.
- Zombies at level 1 (Sean: too easy): 2 outbreak sites (was 1), 4 bitten at each (was 3), turning in 13-21 s (was 20-30); level 2 12-20 s; 5 a site from level 8.
- People stuck in a line (Sean's screenshot): a bus queue member who could not reach the door in 25 s was set to IDLE where they stood, and IDLE stands still for good, so the back of a long queue froze in a line. Now giving up (25 s, or 20 s more waiting at the door, or the bus gone) sends them back to their street (startReturn); people let go by a departing bus who do not flee walk back too. Rioters in custody are let off if their police bus is wrecked or has not arrived 120 s after it was sent.
- No page load on the first trip (Sean's second recording: the sound still died at the first fly-down from the menu): the menu builds no city now. main.js buildWorld() (city, outskirts, traffic, people, fire, helicopters, daylight, disasters, sirens; then worldReady: game.attachWorld, searchlights, remembered damage, window.__z) runs at load for a game in the address (commander, sandbox, be with a kind), and from the menu only when a place is picked: Game.playMode arrive() -> settle(key, mode, extra) (history.replaceState + zday_place, places.js) -> game.buildWorld() under the full cloud cover -> globe.close() (the descent) -> startMode. Same page, so the AudioContext and the music carry on. Game takes its world later (constructor { camera, controls, renderer, mode, post, world }, attachWorld). frame() draws only the globe until there is a world. The menu loads faster (no city). Trips from inside a game (the globe button, On to the next town) still load a page and ask for a tap. The globe no longer calls travelling() on landing; the caller does when it really leaves. Compass and pause hide while the globe is up.
- Trips from inside a game in this page too (Sean: yes, do it): main.js moveWorld(key, mode, kind): keepDamage, game.leaveWorld() (endMode: the mode marked dead so its canvas handlers go quiet (Sandbox hold-to-size, Be driving), its panel, gauge and end screen removed, Follow off; then the world references dropped and the clocks reset), every scene child but the sun, its target and the sky light removed with geometry and materials disposed (the shared textures stay), settle(), setView over the middle, buildWorld(), globe.setHere(). globe.moveTo (main.js) = moveWorld + close (the descent) + startMode in the same mode (Be keeps its disaster) + meteorIn; the globe calls it on landing anywhere else (the 🌍 button, On to <town>, Sandbox Travel, a meteor's direct hit). Commander Play again after a level up = endMode + startMode, no page load. Tested: three Sandbox trips in a row (scene objects steady at 98, one panel, the sound running), Be (tornado kept) and Commander (12 cards) trips. Only the Menu buttons load a page now (the menu taps bring the sound back).
- Rescue vehicles park in a line (Sean: they parked inside each other): traffic.clearOf(car, s) moves the stop back behind any parked mission vehicle in the same lane (same a -> b segment), HALF_LEN per kind + 1.5 m gap; stopAt (the tap point, the crossing for the heavy ones) and park (stuck in traffic) both use it. Tested: 5 units to one spot, all parked, 1.5 m+ apart in a lane.
- Toxic spill (Sean: the zombie outbreak did not play right; a toxic truck crash releasing gas that turns people): spill.js Spill, disaster type spill. A tanker (red cab, steel tank, green band, hazard diamonds) comes 75 m down the nearest street at 28 m/s wobbling, crashes (crash + boom sounds, a burst, people scared 30 m), jackknifes 0.65 rad and rolls onto its side sliding 7 m, then a green cloud (sprites every 0.09 s drifting out, rising and growing) spreads to 24 m in 8 s, holds, thins over the last 6 of 30 s; every 0.5 s civilians in it are infected at 22% (panic and run; turn in inc seconds), up to max. A warn event (hazard zombies) names the district. The wreck stays as a leftover until reset. Commander: the first site of a zombie incident is a spill (max 10 + 3 x seeds, the level's incubation); later sites are bitten people who fled it. Sandbox: Toxic spill · 3 (each tap crashes a tanker there, max 26). Follow disaster names it Toxic spill. Tested: 7 infected at 8 s, 8 zombies at 20 s, 23 at 40 s.
- Heroes landing inside roof huts (Sean: his head was poking out of a building): many roofs carry a stair hut (2.3-3.1 m with a parapet) and the heroes landed at roof height wherever their spot fell, often inside it. city.roofPoint(k, x, z): a spot inside the roof edge and out of the hut (out of its nearest side still on the roof; on a small roof, the corner furthest from it). Used by the hero signal's spot, the heroes resting on it, their drop-off point for people pulled from the water, and Weaver's perch (villains spot). Checked on all 331 roofs with a hut in Pine Hollow: none inside, none off the roof.
- Lives, phase 1 (Sean: NPCs with homes, jobs, schedules, personalities, deciding for themselves; the world should feel alive; revert if it lags): lives.js Lives (people.lives). Per person: home and work (building indices with a street door), archetype (people.reset stores it), six traits 0-255 (fear, greed, loyal, curious, brave, social: an even 0.5 + the archetype's lean (BIAS) + a personal spread), done (worked today), nextT. Homes: a building on the block they walk round (travellers: a tall one, a hotel; the homeless none). Jobs by archetype (JOB): business offices (15 m+), construction anywhere, nurses and students the biggest 8% (clinics, schools), 60% of casual people anything; shoppers, travellers, off-duty officers none; preferably 50-230 m from home. The hour: game.update sets people.hour from the mode's day phase (6 + ((phase - 0.9) mod 1) x 24); people.danger = a disaster on (not heroes, not the spill: an outbreak creeps up on people).
  - Lives.tick (every 0.25 s, an eighth of the people, so each thinks every 2 s): strollers (WALK) decide 40% of the time (else stroll on); crowd regulars (IDLE, index >= nWalk) leave 0.8% of thinks. decide(): utilities from the hour (night 21-6, work 7.5-17, lunch 11.5-13.5, evening 17-21) and traits, + random 0-0.35, best taken: work (once a day), home, shop (a low building 30-170 m), hang (a crowd area; a regular back to their own spot, where they turn IDLE again), stroll. Dwell: work 45-90 s, home 25-50 (night 60-120), shop 10-25, hanging 25-70. No new errands while there is danger; those indoors stay in.
  - Trips: state GO (30, a civilian on foot: CIV, INHASH, HITTABLE, GRABBABLE; walk anim, standing (fx 8 hanging, fx 7 at a kerb)). route(): corner to corner along the pavements (each person on their own line, lat x 0.6, so corners do not crowd), crossing at corners, east-west then north-south or the other way if that crosses a missing street (walkable samples); goStep: brisk (x1.2, x1.7 on the road), sidestepping when blocked, skipping a corner after 5 s without headway, giving up after 280 s; at a kerb they wait (up to 8 s) while a moving car is about to reach the crossing (clearToCross). Arrival: in through the door (INSIDE with fx 9: the routine, hidden, counted alive, crushed or burnt with the building like shelterers; out of the door when the time is up and on to the next thing) unless the building is gone, burning or broken into. The calm-civilian checks (bus calls, alarms, crowds of onlookers) include GO.
  - Measured: Metro City (5,200 walkers + 4,800 in crowds) 1.68 ms a people step with lives, 1.50 without; crowds settle at about 60% (regulars coming and going), a few % of those on the way stuck at once (they skip on). Calm Sandbox: nobody run over (as without). Commander outbreaks: fewer dead with lives (some indoors), more run over (more people out crossing while panicking drivers race about): the next phase (decisions in danger) should take people off the streets.
- Lives, phase 2: decisions in danger (lives.respond, a utility choice like the day's): flee (0.45 + fear 0.5, +0.35 within 8 m, +0.3 where buildings are the danger), fight (zombies, not outnumbered: brave 0.9 - fear 0.5, +0.45 for the old FIGHTER temperament), steal a car (old DRIVER +0.45, greed, fear, - loyal), pray (old FAITHFUL +0.7, a circle nearby), shelter (the nearest refuge within 45 m: 0.35 + timid + fear, +0.45 for indoor-safe kinds, +0.15 zombies), home (within 260 m, not for quakes or floods: 0.2 + loyal 0.7 - distance), loot (greedy over 0.6, 18 m+ from the danger, in a riot, a disaster or an outbreak: 0.25 + greed - fear - loyal, +0.3 in a riot; any building: they all have shopfronts), freeze (within 10 m, fearful and timid: 1-3 s rooted, then they run), up (floods and tsunamis: a roof, trySeek) + noise 0.25. Kinds: INDOORS (tornado, haboob, coldwave, solar, aliens, nuclear, meltdown, volcano, villains, riot, meteor, blackhole, spill), OPEN (quake, sinkhole, kaiju, worm: no sheltering, keener to run), UP (tsunami, flood). disasters.poiAt tags the point with its type. Plans (lives.plan): steps [act, building], e.g. loot the shop then home; GO with plan:true; people.panic leaves someone with a plan alone unless the danger is within 5 m; indoors as fx 9 with lives.after holding the rest (looters come out after 3-6 s with an item (people.looted: kit ITEM, stats.looted, a looting message), then go on home; shelterers stay in while there is danger). people.react became fightBack and stealCar; the tail of panic is people.fleeFrom. Lives.tick in danger: sense() lets anyone within 0.75 of the nearest disaster's range decide (every 20-35 s each); running only from what is close. Onlookers film by curiosity and fear (watchScan).
  - Measured in Metro Sandbox, 30 s after: tornado 1,082 heading in or home, 3,000 sheltering, 576 filming; riot 14 looting (15 done), 132 off to shelter or home; quake nobody sheltering, some praying and stealing cars. Commander outbreak: run over 17 (70 with phase 1 alone). About 3 ms a full game frame in Metro.
  - Headless screenshots: scratchpad shot.cjs drives Playwright's chromium_headless_shell-1248 with SwiftShader (the cached playwright-core expects 1247, so executablePath is given); one at a time, several at once time out.
- Lives, phase 3: friends, enemies, memory, trust (Sean: NPCs who form relationships, remember, respond to reputation). lives.relate() at reset: households (each home's residents shuffled into groups of 1-4: 20/35/25/20%), two friends each (f1 from work, f2 from their block, else anyone within 160 m, never their own household) and an enemy (foe) for 12% (someone within 160 m). Pines: 4,145 people, 1,757 households, 8,277 friend links, 464 enemies.
  - Day: friends who pass within 3.5 m while free (WALK, or GO on their own errand, not on a plan) stop and talk 3-8 s, facing (pause(): a GO trip at:true fx 8 holding the trip they were on in resume, picked up after); 45 s before they chat again. Enemies within 4 m have words (3-6 s; brave + greed - loyal > 0.45 shoves the other 0.9 m; first one a message: argue). The sociable bring a free friend within 60 m to the shops or the park (bring(), chance social/255). Evenings and nights, home pulls 0.3 harder when someone of their household is in (famHome). Pines quiet morning: about 600 chats and a few arguments in 2 minutes.
  - Danger (respond): gather (one of their people outside, within 60 m and within 25 m of the danger: 0.1 + loyal 0.8 + brave 0.3 - fear 0.35): a GATHER trip that follows them (re-routes if they move 8 m), then both to the nearest refuge together. police (trust > 0.35 and an officer within 50 m: 0.25 + trust 0.8 + (1 - brave) 0.15): a POLICE trip to beside the officer, 8-16 s standing there, then the nearest refuge. Looting + (0.6 - trust) 0.5.
  - Memory (mourn, each think): one of their people (household first, then friends; up to 6, a bit each in lost) dead, a zombie or turning: the brave (> 165) get braver (+20), the rest more afraid (fear +30). Within 40 m: a brave one (> 150) within 15 m of what their friend became goes for it (fightBack); otherwise they stop and grieve 4-8 s (first one a message: mourn).
  - Trust (lives.trust 0.05-0.98, starts 0.6; trustTick once a second from stats changes): + zombies shot 0.0015, rescued 0.004, bused 0.002, arrests 0.003, treated 0.003, jailed 0.001; - civilians bombed 0.02, nuked 0.003, other civilian dead 0.0012 (dead minus shot, bombed, nukedZ, aliensKilled), 0.001 a second while over 60 zombies; drifts 0.4% a second back to 0.6. Effects: callToBus skips people at 0.35 + trust 0.65 (65% at 0.5), alarmAt (the police roof order in a tsunami) 0.4 + trust 0.6, the police option and looting above. Commander HUD: a Trust pill (red under 35%, green over 70%).
  - Lives drops the trips of the dead and the evacuated. Measured, Pines Commander outbreak with two cruisers and a bus: 30 zombies shot, contained, trust 0.60 -> 0.65, 22 stayed by officers, 12 bused; left alone it falls to about 0.1 as the city is overrun. Full game frame about 1 ms there.
- Lives, phase 4: a card for anyone you tap (folk.js Folk, game.folk). With no unit picked (Commander), no disaster picked (Sandbox; not in Be The Disaster, where the finger drives), a tap within 26 px of someone (each person projected where the curve draws them, 0.4 ms for Pines; not when the camera is over 650 m out) opens their card in the bar (order 0, above the chips; the side buttons and compass hide while it is up, body.zd-person-on). A tap on empty ground closes it. Modes say busy() when a tap is theirs.
  - Who: names from 88 first and 76 last names by the person's number and the town seed (a household shares the first member's surname, four in five); age by kind (students 15-25, retired over 64); job by kind (office titles, trades on a building site, hospital staff, school or college, casual jobs with their place: barista at a café, mechanic at a garage...), with the district of their work and home (disasters.district). Officers, soldiers and firefighters get rank and surname; aliens just Alien.
  - Card rows: Now (from the state and the trip: chatting with / arguing with / grieving for a named person, on the way to work or school, running home, going to get someone, at home, in class, sheltering, filming, praying, a zombie now...), Mood (word and a meter: calm, cheerful, uneasy, scared, furious, heartbroken, shaken, terrified, hungry), Nature (their two strongest leanings, plus fighter / never far from a car / devout), Home (district, who they live with), Friends (workmate, classmate, neighbour or friend), Can't stand, Lost. Every name is a link (red if dead, turned or bitten): the card and the view go to them. Show flies there; Follow (followSubject kind person: 110 m back, looking down into the street; the follow stays on them out to 60 m past the town's edge; inside, the camera waits at the door) and Stop following. A pale ring pulses on the ground round them. The card refreshes every 0.4 s. lives.pause() now records who with and what (chat, argue, grieve).
- Outbreak music (Sean: the action music did not match a zombie outbreak): music.js plays the clean-up track (rebuild.mp3) while there are zombies or a tanker spill, the storm track only for any other disaster (with or without an outbreak), clean-up for 150 s after, then calm.
- Spill zombies glow (Sean: level 1's tanker zombies should be the glowing green ones; plain zombies kept for another disaster): the gas sets zrad on those it infects (radioactive type when they turn: green skin, glow, 2 hits, the 6.5 m aura), a bite from a radioactive zombie passes zrad on, and Commander's later outbreak sites (infectAt rad 1) glow too ("someone glowing green is biting people!"). Pines level 1, two cruisers: held, 34 shot, two officers lost.
- More police cars (Sean: if I have the points I should be able to use them): traffic MISSION_KINDS police cap 12 -> 48 (the instanced mesh's size), MISSION_CAP 24 -> 72 vehicles out at once; traffic.full(kind) for the toast (All 48 cruisers are out). Metro, 48 cruisers parked with 86 officers: about 3 ms a frame.
- Quarantine and the mini nuke (Sean: box a zombie outbreak in with police at the right streets, then a mini nuke clears it; any that get out need boxing in and nuking again; the mini nuke makes no new zombies). quarantine.js Quarantine (commander.quar), stepped from Commander.update.
  - Grid: 4 m cells over the town (+40 m), walkable if the people grid is walkable at the centre or 1.3 m off it. Blocked, once a second: round each stopped or parked cruiser's post (car.post = the spot tapped; 12 m, junction and all) and a line across the street it parked in (both ways out to the buildings, up to 15 m, 3 lines thick); officers and soldiers in COP state within 25 m of their post hold 8.5 m round the post. Cruiser officers now take their posts at the spot tapped (if within 40 m of where the car stopped).
  - Zones: zombies (ZOMBIE, TURNING, PILE, CLIMB; on a roof or piled against a building: counted at its door; one inside a roadblock is left to the police) flood out over walkable unblocked cells. Sealed: boxed in and at most 2,500 cells (about 200 m across); anything bigger counts as open (a whole small town is boxed in by its countryside). Open zones: the streets out are where a flood from their zombies reaches 9 cells (36 m), merged within 18 m, up to 16: pulsing amber rings (48 at most). Sealed zones: red fill (0.2) and a red edge ribbon, 0.7 m up. 0.6 ms a look in Metro.
  - While there are zombies, officers hold their posts: they leave a target more than 24 m from the post and do not roam to mop up (people.holdPosts).
  - Messages: once, how it works (police on every amber ring, then a mini nuke); sealed (2+ zombies, a spot not told in 30 s); broke out (a sealed zone open again). The radio's all-clear waits until no zombies or bitten are left (it said the worst was over below 16).
  - Mini nuke card (150 pts, from level 1, second in the row): picks only while a zone is sealed (else it says to box them in); a tap must be inside a red zone (an open one says how many streets are still open). people.nukeIf(test) (nuke() now uses it) burns everyone in the zone's cells or a cell beyond them that no roadblock holds (the cordon is spared), indoors too by their building's door; civilians count as nuked (points and trust, as the big nuke). Buildings in it are charred and will not burn. A small mushroom cloud, flash, shake and the nuke sound (nukeflash). No radiation, no doses: nobody turns from it.
  - Pines, six turned in one street, police sent to each amber ring: sealed on the second round (33 cells, 12 zombies), the nuke cleared it; four had got out first and grew to 32, to be boxed in again. Metro: sealed on the third round.
- Getting people out of a nuke zone (Sean: extra points if you get people out; anyone in the nuke zone dies): Quarantine.track() (each look, while a zone is sealed) counts the healthy civilians in each sealed zone (in its streets, or in or on a building whose door opens onto it) and remembers each one with that zone's spot. The mini nuke kills everyone in it, roofs and indoors included (its buildings: door or middle in the zone); each remembered person from that zone who is now out of it (on a bus or in a stolen car, or past the police), alive and unbitten, scores +5, and +50 more if nobody was left inside. The sealed message says how many people are in there and to get them out (an evac bus). Errands no longer lead into a sealed zone (lives.go asks quar.closed(); running in to fetch family, sheltering and going to the police still can). Pines test: 9 boxed in, a bus took 11 off, 15 got out (+75), 17 still inside died.
- See-through walls (Sean: keep the action visible, see through buildings): see.js seeThrough(mat) patches the city's facade, shop and roof materials (they already carry their world position, vHoleW, for the ground holes). Each frame (main.js seeFrame) the line from the camera to what it looks at (the view's target, or game.focusAt while following) is a tube; wall and roof fragments within it (radius dist x 0.07, 9-24 m, a little wider at the action end), between the camera and the action only (not behind it), are discarded in a stipple (interleaved gradient noise, up to 86%), so a ghost of the building stays and the tilt-shift blur softens it. Fades in from 430 m to 330 m camera distance. Shadows unchanged. window.__z.see.on = false switches it off for comparisons. Metro test (tallest building's street, an outbreak behind the block in front): before, hidden; after, the zombies, cars and blood in plain view.
- Fix (c3d1abf): Quarantine.closed() ran before the grid existed (lives.go on the first errand) and stopped Commander's game loop; it now returns false until a zone exists.
- Playtest round 1 (Sean: have cheaper agents play both modes at 8x, make the game beatable start to end, fix what they find). Two Sonnet agents on frozen builds (scratchpad playtest-build on :4300, playtest-build2 on :4301; launch entries playtest and playtest2), scripted human-like play through g.mode.select/tap. Commander: Red Mesa (town 1, level 1) never won in about 20 rounds. Changes from it:
  - Freeze: People.boardStep read a bus door that had gone (the bus left mid-boarding) and stopped the game loop; they step down and head home now.
  - Mini nuke bonus: tracked by each person's own cell in the sealed zone and when (60 s expiry), counted only if that cell is in the nuked zone (adjacent zones and the town-sized zones had paid thousands of points). zoneAt falls back only to a zone cell within 2 cells of the tap. Flash 0.5 (1.2 washed the picture out).
  - Officers hold their posts only while more than 12 zombies are left; then they hunt the stragglers (one zombie in a sealed zone had stopped a round ending as contained).
  - Population: the 320 corner crowd groups (about 2,400 people in every town) scale with the town's people factor (x 0.6 on low quality): Red Mesa had 34 people per 1,000 m2 against Metro's 17.
  - Level 1-4 outbreaks: one site at level 1 (2 at 2-4, 3 from 5); the tanker infects 8 at level 1 (12, 16, 22 later; was 22); incubation 24-34 s at level 1, 18-28 s at 2, 14-22 s at 3-4, 10-18 s from 5. Tanker-gas zombies (zrad 1) glow but drop to one shot and do not infect without a bite; nuclear ones (zrad 2: nuke doses, the radiation aura) keep 2 hp and the aura. START_RP 100 -> 250.
  - Levels follow the road in Commander: Profile.level = max(XP level, furthest town opened + 1) while profile.mode is set (Commander sets it, endMode clears it); a town win that raises it shows Level up. XP alone (12 levels to 520,000) would have left the whole road at level 1-3.
  - Amber rings snap to the nearest junction within 22 m (one cruiser closes every street through it), the same junction once.
- Playtest round 2 (Commander, build :4301): the road was beaten end to end in one chained run (Mesa 3 stars, every other town 2, four retries, no losses, no crashes in about 100 rounds; level 11 at Metro). Changes from it:
  - Lone piles: a pile of one or two with nobody on the roof (people only indoors) gives up after 20 s and falls back into the street, where it can be shot (piled zombies are lvl -2, out of the officers' reach; one had held rounds open to 600 s). No overrun when a pile gives up (collapse(k, gaveUp)).
  - City secured is no longer held up by a disaster on its way out (commander spent(e): leaving, kaiju state leave, worm phase leave/gone, every villain beaten or off) or by two or fewer buildings still burning. Commander starts the long incidents shorter (LIFE: villains 240 s, kaiju 200, worm 160, aliens 140). Fire trucks: cap 8 -> 14.
  - Floods and tsunamis: being swept away kills 10% outright (was 25%), and those afloat last 60-120 s (was 25-80): Harbor flood, no response, 522 drowned (was about 1,245) with 1,057 afloat at 96 s for boats and helicopters to reach. Flood barrier unlocks at level 4 (tsunamis come at 4; it was 5).
  - Toasts smaller, lighter and higher (112 px, 12 px type): they covered the middle of the action. Lakeside people 0.26 -> 0.34, Sun Valley 0.32 -> 0.4, so each town on the road is bigger than the last.
  - Still little counterplay (noted for later): cold wave, haboob, kaiju without heroes.
- Be The Disaster playtest round 1 (all 21 disasters played on the road; the first 10 towns cleared, Metro only by four). Changes from it:
  - Hang: Traffic.head started zero-filled, so Traffic.near() looped forever if anyone looked for cars before the first buildHash (Bayview every time: lives.goStep, clearToCross, on the first step; Commander and Sandbox could too). It starts at -1 and is cleared on reset.
  - The nuclear heat wash held the screen at the hottest it had ever been until the blast was disposed; it is the hottest this frame now, so it fades.
  - Ranks are against the town's goal (30 a building): F, D 0.25x, C 0.6x, B 1x, A 1.6x, S 2.5x (fixed scores made every small town S and Metro C). Score factors: earthquake 1 -> 0.6, meteors 0.45 -> 0.3, volcano 2.5 -> 1.9, nuclear 0.2 -> 0.16, dust storm 0.62 -> 0.9, tsunami 0.35 -> 0.5, tripod 3.5 -> 5.5, meltdown 1.4 -> 3, kaiju 0.9 -> 3. Order: earthquake level 2 -> 6, meteors 3 -> 8, volcano 7 -> 9. XP per run capped at 9,500 (score 95,000). Its level follows its road like Commander's (profile.mode 'be'; the menus show each mode's level).
  - Dust storm: the next wall comes once the last is 60% across (it waited 50 s after each had gone), and only the newest follows the finger: storms 2 and 3 now come, about 29,600 at Lakeside (was about 20,400). Flood: water over 2.5 m takes the lowest of a dozen buildings, about one every few seconds deeper (158 of 431 at Lakeside; it scored nothing after a minute).
  - Saucer: keeps beaming within 16 m of the finger (was 8), beams 10 m round, and drops those it is lifting only after 0.8 s off the beam.
  - Endings: the last town (Metro) says the road is done, in both modes (2+ stars in Commander, the goal in Be The Disaster).
  - See-through per mode: Commander and Sandbox as before; in Be The Disaster only for the ones that are someone in the streets (saucer, tripod, zombies, riot, villains, kaiju, worm), out to 600 m (its camera sits further back). It speckled the very buildings a tornado was wrecking.
  - Still open: some disasters' camera does not follow (tsunami, lava, the earthquake's fault tip); floods and dust storms read as brown haze; the side buttons sit on the playfield.
- Commander playtest round 3: the road beaten again (13 rounds, 10 contained, was 7); hard towns 9 of 20 at 2+ stars (was 7); bonus farm confirmed closed; no hangs. Changes from it:
  - Small piles: buildingTick lists piles of 1-3 (people.smallPiles); an officer with nothing to shoot within 22 m knocks one of them down (unpile: back to ZOMBIE in the street) and shoots it. The 20 s give-up only covered empty roofs, and the stuck ones were under roofs with survivors.
  - Incidents come closer together: 4 + k x 260/(n-1) s (was 420): the playtester's test over 20 hard-town rounds gave 14 at 2+ stars (was 9) and 6 holdouts (was 15).
  - Fires: City secured allows up to 4% of buildings still burning (a volcano left dozens); stars: secured with under 45% alive is 1 star (it was 2 at any survival).
  - Rescue: boats and helicopters go for the swimmer nearest to going under (distance + seconds left x 1.2; on a raft they wait): rescue barely changed deaths before.
  - Nuclear and meltdown hordes grew without end: the nuclear kind's aura 5% -> 2% a half second, nuclear doses 12% -> 7%, meltdown fallout 0.02 -> 0.012 x strength.
- Commander playtest round 4: road beaten again; hard towns 13 of 20 at 2+ stars (was 9), 6 holdouts (was 15); lone piles gone; meltdown winnable (3 stars with a response). Changes from it:
  - The last few: with three or fewer zombies left, any with nobody to chase head for the nearest officer (people.cops, listed each tally) like zombies drawn by gunfire: officers walk straight at their targets and could not get round a block to the last two or three, which held rounds open for minutes.
  - The nuclear strike in a Commander round is smaller with a longer warning (size 170, was 280; warning 30 s, was 20): Harbor, 20% dead 90 s in (it was 44% in the first minute, so 2 stars were out of reach).
  - Still: floods, tsunamis and cold waves cost 25-35% whatever is sent (rescue moves it 3-10%); three barriers cut tsunami deaths 11-13%.
- Commander playtest round 5: road beaten (every town first try but Metro, whose first try hit the crash below); hard towns 15 of 20 at 2+ stars (was 13), none lost; Metro 3 of 4 (was 1). Changes from it:
  - Crash: a flood starting while a tsunami was coming in cleared the water front under it, and WaterSurface.frontAt read null. frontAt returns far ahead when there is no front, and a flood leaves a live tsunami's front alone.
  - The incident banner and the toast sat in the same place; while a banner is up the toast drops below it (game.placeToast).
  - Officers reach for a small pile from 160 m once only a few zombies are left (22 m before).
  - Nuclear rounds: the strike now kills 10-38% (was 44%), but a sensible bot still only holds out at 1 star (the fallout zombies keep growing); a player who seals ground zero fast may do better. Meltdown is winnable.
- Be The Disaster playtest round 2: the road beaten in 11 runs from a fresh profile, every town on the first try (no grinding: level follows the road); Metro cleared by 13 of 21 (was 4); Bayview fixed; the nuclear wash fades; see-through clean. Changes from it:
  - Ranks: against a bar of goal x (2167 / buildings)^0.4 (the win line stays the goal): one finger reaches every street of a hamlet but not of a city, so scores grow with about the 0.4 power of the town (19 of 21 were S at Red Mesa, none at Metro).
  - The ones short of Metro's goal: zombies 2.2 -> 4, riot 7 -> 13, lava 1.6 -> 2.6, solar 1 -> 1.25, tripod 5.5 -> 7, worm 1.3 -> 1.8, kaiju 3 -> 5.5, villains 1 -> 1.8.
  - The camera rides the tsunami and dust fronts (follow() through game.front: the view's point on the front; it stayed put and lost them). A warhead every 30 s (was 60: long dead stretches). The Be list says which town to clear to unlock each disaster.
  - Still: the dust storm reads as brown haze; flat tails once a small town is used up; building collapses cost 20-30 ms steps.
- Be The Disaster playtest round 3: 18 of 21 clear Metro (13 before, 4 at first); the road in 11 runs, every town first try; tsunami follow good. Last changes: the rank bar exponent 0.4 -> 0.6 (Mesa still ranked a step above Metro); worm 1.8 -> 2, black hole 1.4 -> 1.55, meltdown 3 -> 3.3 (just under or on the line), kaiju 5.5 -> 4.2 (it topped Metro). The dust storm was solid brown: in Be The Disaster each storm is light (Haboob opts.light: no dark dust fog after the wall, a wall 150 m high not 280, sand weather), and the camera rides 40 m ahead of the wall and swings round to stand ahead of it facing it (BE.update uses ctl.heading()), so the wall is seen rolling in over the streets.
- Kaiju pacing: the hero signal waits until the kaiju is 15 s old (response.js); Heroes.holds adds a 12 s hold while a kaiju is up (heroes arrive about 28 s in, was 4 s). Tempo's kaiju bubble: 5 s every 24 s (was 6 s every 9 s); between bubbles he punches the heads with the others (doMonster switches to the slow job when cd is ready). Tested: kaiju slowed 19% of its time (about a quarter of the fight), driven off at 110 s.

- Arcade, 2026-10-28 (Sean: "not very fun, but I love watching the destruction"; he picked hit rewards, shorter runs, chain reactions, landmark targets, mixed disasters, then Disaster TV, one at a time):
  - 1. Hit rewards (src/hits.js, Be The Disaster only): each building down, building set alight and car wrecked (each car once: traffic events now carry car/other) pops up its points over where it happened (gathered per 45 m patch every 0.25 s, 18 pooled divs, drawn on the curved ground). Hits within 3.5 s build a combo: x1.5 at 6 hits, x2 at 16, x2.5 at 32, x3 at 56; a burst counts at most about 3 hits a second (tokens), so a nuke or meteor does not max it at once. The bonus (points x (mult - 1)) is added to the score. The multiplier and a draining bar sit on the score pill; the score rolls up and bumps.
  - Named moments (big words mid-screen, a stinger sound, a jolt): Big one! (one of the tallest 4% in the town), Whole block! (7 buildings in 1.5 s), Pile-up! (7 cars in 2 s), Up in flames! (6 fires in 4 s); each at most every 6 s. Big one and Whole block also give a beat of slow motion (game.slowT: the world at 0.3 speed, at most every 7 s). Go! at the start, 10 seconds! and Time! at the end; the end card lists the best combo and the combo bonus. Sounds: chime (combo steps) and stinger, synthesized in audio.js. Nothing pops up over people (the score still counts victims as before).

  - 2. Shorter runs: Be The Disaster runs 90 s (RUN, was 180); the end card comes up 1.7 s after Time! (was 2.5) with Again on it. Bot runs at 90 s with combos (Metro, Harbor): the building-wreckers score about what they did in 180 s before (tornado 91k, quake 129k, meteor 92k at Metro); the people ones fell short, so: zombies start with 5 bites (was 3), one back every 3 s (was 5) up to 8, 6 bitten a bite (was 4), k 4 -> 6; meltdown blows after 5 s held (was 8), k 3.3 -> 4.5; riot 13 -> 15.5, flood 0.8 -> 1.15, blizzard 4 -> 7, saucer 3.5 -> 4.2, tripod 7 -> 8.2. The goal stays 30 a building.
  - 3. Chain reactions (src/chain.js, Be The Disaster only): a building 9 m+ coming down (the collapse event now carries `fall`, the angle debris 'sink' tips it by; its top leans toward (-sin, cos)) brings down the nearest building within ~40 degrees of that lean and within its reach 60% of the time (20% if that one is much taller), 0.55-0.9 s later, leaning on down the line, up to 5 deep, 3 in the air at once: Domino!. A burning car blows 4-9 s after catching (a burst, a boom, people scared off, nobody hurt by it): parked cars within 7 m catch and blow in turn, moving cars within 8 m are thrown (soft: nobody under them) and may land burning, a building within 5 m may catch; 3 booms in 8 s: Chain reaction!. A building burning 10 s gets one 30% roll at its gas main (one every 4 s at most): fire.blast round it (its 'blast' event dropped so a kaiju does not take it as a hit), it comes down: Gas main!. Tested: Metro quake 6 dominoes, 12 car booms in 47 s; Metro riot 5 booms and a gas main; worst 4-step frame 47-62 ms headless.
  - 4. Landmark targets (src/targets.js): three per town, picked at each run's start: its landmarks first (City hall, Stadium, Art Deco tower; never the glass Skyscraper), then the tallest and the biggest buildings, interleaved, at least 22% of the town's width apart (20 m if that leaves fewer than three); named Tallest building / Biggest building / High-rise / Big block. A gold star with its name over each, or at the edge of the view with an arrow when out of sight (kept clear of the HUD, the round buttons and the bar). One down: '<name> down!' with slow motion, a star in the HUD pill and a bonus of 10% of the town's goal (hits.pop shows it); all three: All three!. The end card lists Landmarks with stars.
  - 5. Mixed disasters (src/mix.js): 30 s into a run three cards come up over the bar (Add a second disaster, 7 s countdown; too slow and one is picked): tornado (an AI one 130-180 m off, life 45 s), earthquake (mag 6.9 nearby), meteor shower (3 rocks, size 11-17, 3 s apart), sinkhole (22 m), lightning storm (26 s of strikes on roofs round the action, which may set them alight); never the player's own kind. It runs on its own near the player's disaster (be.at()). Scoring: while one is loose (marked extra; plus 8 s after, 12 s from the pick) every unit of wrecking scores at min(k, 1) (be.rate(): points now accumulate half second by half second at the rate of the moment). Tallying it apart leaked (deaths are recounted from states every half second; collapses come later off fires and timers): a riot (k 15.5) plus meteors made 175k in Harbor against a 19k goal; capped, riot + meteors is 43k at 40 s against 22k alone. End card: Second disaster. Also: zombies k 6 -> 7, worm 2 -> 2.4, kaiju 4.2 -> 4.8 (90 s bot runs: 0.91, 0.81, 0.86 of Metro's goal; the rest of the retuned ones 1.06-1.33).
  - 6. Disaster TV (src/tv.js, ?mode=tv, the fourth mode on the menu, which now lays its modes out two by two; from the menu it starts in a random town): four disasters a town from a shuffled pool (tornado, quake, flood, tsunami, riot, aliens, meteor shower via mix.startExtra, sinkhole, volcano, dust storm, cold wave, solar flare, black hole, villains, worm, kaiju; villains/kaiju/worm/aliens given lives of 70-80 s), the next 5 s after one is over or after 60 s anyway (they can overlap), then on to a random other town (journey). The follow director (game.followSubject) takes the newest one as soon as it is somewhere to look at (villains and alien machines matched through who/tripods/saucers). A LIVE badge and MLD NEWS · <town>, a red NEWS ticker crawling over the bar (BREAKING headlines, the disasters' own warnings instead of toasts, a tally every 20 s: buildings down, cars wrecked, fires), Next up / Next stop cards. Hits in quiet mode (no numbers or combos, only the named moments and slow motion) and chain reactions. Buttons: Next disaster, Next town, Menu. Tested at Harbor and Northgate: black hole, kaiju, villains, tornado, aliens in turn, no errors; the kaiju's first appearance costs one ~400 ms hitch (its module loads).
