- See-through switch (Sean asked for one): an eye in the column of round buttons (see.js seeButton, under the radio): ON/OFF, remembered in localStorage zday_see; SEE.user is the player's switch, ANDed with the mode's SEE.on in seeFrame.

- Hero and villain colours (Sean: dark for villains, neon for heroes, each their own): heroes.js kit().look gives each hero a neon suit, boots and cape with an emissive glow (Dragonfly #3dff5c green, Nova #ff3fcf pink, Tempo #29e3ff cyan; belt and badge stay gold); villains.js vmats(name) gives each villain a dark suit and cape (Blur maroon #7a1229, Tyrant deep purple #4d1672, Weaver bottle green #155a33; skin and hair as before). The close-up smooth body adds a material's emissive to its vertex colour so the neon holds up close. The roster shows them.

- Hand to hand (src/melee.js, Brawl.melee; Sean: more hand-to-hand, villains defend themselves and team up on one hero, punches and kicks, laser against laser with a shock wave):
  - Bouts: hero and villain square up 2.6 m apart (feet at the villain's height; flyers come down to it) and trade blows every 0.5-0.7 s: punch 55%, kick 30%, uppercut 15%, each a pose laid over the figure's own (fightPose, called in Hero.sync and each villain's sync before tumble): guard, punch with a torso twist, kick, uppercut, block, reel. A blow lands 45% into its swing: blocked (villains 38%, a hero 30%, a hero alone against two 15%: sparks, a slap) or a hit (a burst, a thud, knocked back a step, villains lose 14/18/24 hp through Brawl.hurt, so a worn-down villain is beaten). After 5-8 blows a finisher (kick or uppercut) sends the loser flying (toss): villains lose 58% one on one, 75% when outnumbered; a hero alone against two loses 75%.
  - Who starts them: heroes, up close, 45% of the time (Nova on Tyrant, Dragonfly on Weaver; 35% on a held or dazed Blur), before grabs, drags and single punches; villains turn on any free hero within 12 m (70% a scan, every 0.4 s), Blur breaking off a run to do it. A second villain within 150 m piles into a one-on-one (50% a scan after 1.5 s): '<A> and <B> are ganging up on <hero>!', one each side of the hero; then a free hero within 130 m may fly in to help (40%), two on two as pairs side by side. Never a villain the player drives, one carrying something, Weaver swinging, or anyone thrown or held.
  - Beam clash: Tyrant's red eyes against Nova's pink eyes or Tempo's cyan hands, 25-95 m apart (Tyrant's scan 20%, or Nova instead of fetching a car, 35%), 25 s apart at least: both hold 34-60 m apart at 28 m+ up, facing; the meeting point is shoved back and forth (28-72% along), a white core in a glow that grows, sparks, rising shake; after 3.2-4.4 s it bursts: a ring of force out to ~57 m and a shell, a boom and a flash, both thrown back 30 m/s, Tyrant -40 hp, windows out in buildings within 40 m that reach up to it, cars within 26 m knocked aside, people scared off.
  - Tested in the Sandbox at Northgate, 150 s at 4x: 26 bouts, 7 gang-ups, 4 heroes coming to help, 3 clashes, 211 blows, worst 4-step frame 53 ms, no errors; Be The Disaster villains (player driven) unaffected.

- The big moves (src/stunts.js, Brawl.stunts; Sean asked for both):
  - Sky slam: 40% of bout finishers. An uppercut sends the loser up 75 m (70-150 above the street) over a second; the winner flies up after to 7 m above, a hammer blow (both fists down: fightPose 'hammer'), the victim falls at 160 m/s2 with the striker diving after. On the street: cracks and a scour, a ring of dust, a ring of force out to 55 m, a boom and a jolt; buildings under 20 m within 6 m come down, windows out to 40 m, cars within 36 m knocked aside, people within 40 m bowled over and running (not hurt by it). The victim lies on their back 2.2 s (downT), a villain loses 60 hp. In 200 s of Sandbox: 9 slams.
  - Space dive (villains, AI only): not in the first 45-75 s of a visit, then one every 100-140 s. Straight up (to 420 m/s, a fiery trail) out of sight; a solid red pulsing ring (radius 44 m) on the ground where they will land, near where they left, people there run, a warning (hazard 'villains' so Commander reports it); 2.2 s later they come back down fist first from off to one side, burning, in 1.3 s; a mini nuke: NukeBlast R 55 with { clean, mini } (no contamination zone, no radiation doses, a smaller flash and shake). The nearest free hero races to the spot 60% of the time and meets them 110 m up: 50% caught (an air burst, the villain thrown aside, -80 hp, no blast), else knocked aside and it comes down anyway.

- Disaster TV cameras (src/tvcam.js; Sean: a helicopter view the disasters can attack, cameras on tall buildings the disaster takes out): the director holds the camera (controls disabled and their distance and angle limits lifted while TV is on, restored on dispose; camera.lookAt each frame, game.focusAt on the action). Cameras: SKY 7 and SKY 4, news helicopters circling the action at about 1.2x the follow camera's own distance for that subject (g.shot / g.disasterShot: far for a kaiju, close for a villain), with a little sway; and up to 5 ROOF CAMs on the tallest buildings, well apart, at the roof corner nearest the middle of town, panning after the action. Cuts every 13-22 s (helicopter and roofs in turn; the roof camera about 170 m from the action). The label shows the camera (and the helicopter's altitude in feet), corner brackets frame the picture.
  - Attacks: after 12 s on air (a helicopter) or 8 s (a roof), a 30-35% chance every 5 s, twice a disaster at most: '<the disaster> spotted SKY 7!' then by kind: a red beam straight at the camera (kaiju, villains, aliens), a glowing lump hurled at it (worm, tornado, volcano, meteor, riot), the signal drowned in static (solar flare, dust storm, cold wave; the black hole drags the picture round), or the building brought down under it (quake, sinkhole, flood, tsunami: roof cameras only). The hit: a burst, the roof camera's building comes down, the picture tumbles and drops, then SIGNAL LOST static for 1.2 s and a cut. A roof camera whose building falls on its own is lost the same way. Lost cameras stay lost in that town; with none left the station sends up another helicopter. The ticker says so ('Our crew is safe'). The radio's captions sit above the ticker in TV.

- Arctic blast (the cold wave; Sean: like the dust storm but white, snow only behind the front): the cold wave's two flat curtains are gone (the curtain code with them); it uses the dust storm's own DustWall (H 190) in white and ice blue (lit #ffffff, shade #b4c3d6, dark #8797ad, pale #f2f6fb) with white powder boiling along its foot. Snow: the precipitation shader takes the front (weather.snowBehind(sw): FRONT uniforms, flakes faded in from 25 m behind the front, none ahead; cleared when the cold breaks or on dispose); the snowy sky (weather 'snow') comes in once the front is 30 m past where you are looking, or has crossed the town; icy roads (gripK 0.35) still from a quarter of the way in. Be The Disaster's Blizzard (a steerable whirl) is unchanged.
- Radio line 8 (aliens) stopped at "It's...": the automatic cut had put "it's not from this world!" (44.72-45.98 s of the recording) at the start of line 9 (the meteor). Re-cut 08.mp3 36.82-46.16 and 09.mp3 46.18-49.50 the same way (-ss before -i, 15 ms in, 80 ms out, 32 kHz mono 64 kbps); both loud to the end in the browser. The files load as ?v=3 so phones fetch them fresh.

- Flood water invisible (Sean, after a tsunami): the water surface was one 1x1-segment sheet; the world's curve (curve.js) bends geometry only at vertices, so zoomed out its far corners dropped tens of metres and the whole sheet sank under the streets. Now 96x96 segments; the cold wave's frost (64x64), the solar flare's glow (64x64) and the meltdown fallout cloud (24x24) got the same. Disaster TV: rooftop shots have a much deeper sharp band (post bandK 0.2, rangeK 0.9, blurFrac 0.011; helicopter bandK x1.6, rangeK x1.4), restored when TV ends; no stinger sound for TV's named moments (hits quiet mode).

- TV camera attacks by villains and the kaiju (Sean: often; cars thrown at the cameras, or right up to them to pick them up): for these two, after 6 s on air a 60% chance every 4 s, up to 4 a disaster (others: 2). 45% throw, 43% grab, 12% beam. Throw: a real car from the street (nearest within 160 m of the attacker) lifted over the villain's head (both hands up; the kaiju holds it 38 m up) in 0.9 s, then hurled at the lens in 1.1 s, spinning, the camera following it in; a crash, the picture drops into SIGNAL LOST. Grab: Tyrant flies up to a helicopter (others throw instead); for a roof camera Tyrant if he is free, else the villain best placed, flies to 13 m in front of the lens (the camera draws nothing nearer than 10 m), the camera watching it come; it reaches out, the picture lifts with it held in frame (the town jolting behind; no tilt), a spin at the end, then 'SKY 7 WAS GRABBED BY TYRANT' / 'ROOF CAM GRABBED'. The kaiju walks over to a roof camera (its goal; no other actions on the way) and takes it within 58 m (it steers round towers over 70 m, where the roof cameras are), 60% bringing the building down; 32 s and still not there, it throws a car instead. The helicopter's orbit holds still while one comes for it.

- Floods (Sean: no water; cars should float): the flood colour was dark brown, lost against the sepia streets: now muddy olive (deep 0.085,0.1,0.085, shallow 0.23,0.25,0.19), a little more sky reflection and moving ripple glints. Floating cars: traffic mode FLOAT (6): every 0.5 s a stalled, wrecked or resting car (not a mission vehicle or a tough one) in water over 1.1 m deep lifts off; it drifts on slow eddies (and traffic.flowAt, a whirlpool's pull), turns, bobs and rolls a little, sits about two thirds under (w - 0.95), knocks off walls (not walkable), and settles (REST) where the water is under 0.7 m. Parked cars in water over 1.1 m float off as debris objects (mode float), five every half second, up to 130 debris objects; debris floating also feels debris.flowAt. Floating cars count as wrecked for Be The Disaster's score. Crossing, a 3.2 m flood: 35 cars and 131 parked cars afloat at 50 s; all aground after it drained.

- Whirlpools (src/whirlpool.js, WaterSurface.pools): a sinkhole or worm hole (leftover Hole, shape 'shaft', not the black hole's void, R > 2.5) under water over 0.55 m deep spins up a whirlpool: a spiral-armed disc 2.8x the hole's radius on the surface with a dark throat (16x16 segments, to bend with the curve), spray round the rim, a warning once ('A whirlpool! The flood is pouring down the hole'). It drains the flood: 0.0045 x hole radius m/s at full strength (a 16 m sinkhole took a 3.2 m flood to 0.84 m in 30 s) through Flood.drained, the tsunami's floodH, Be The Disaster's flood level and W.level (never under 0.35 m). Its pull: traffic.flowAt / debris.flowAt give a swirl velocity (drawn in about as fast as it turns, so things spiral in; floating things follow it as a velocity field, or they orbit like moons) and within 0.85 of the hole's radius cars (traffic.remove) and wreckage go down with a splash; over the throat the pit's rim is not a wall. Swimmers within it are drawn round and in, and pulled under in the throat (kill 'drown'); people wading under 0.8 m keep their feet. It dies away as the water gets shallow.

- Volcano under water (Volcano.boilStep): erupting where the water is over 0.4 m deep, the water boils over a ring widening from 30 + 40k m by 2.2 m/s (to 170 sqrt(k)): the surface shader's uHot pales and froths it with bursting bubbles; spray bursts all over; steam: a column over the vent up to 70 sqrt(k) + 20 m and drifting wisps off the patch (fire.mist; kept thin: thick steam whited out the screen); 'The flood water is boiling over the volcano! Get out of the water'. Every 0.5 s anyone in the water (over 0.3 m) inside it dies 55% of the time ('burn'); floating cars in it blister black and after 3 s steam and sink (traffic.remove). Lava bombs landing in deep water burst in steam and scald those within 5 m. The boil fades when the water drains or the eruption ends. Crossing, a 3 m flood: 140-160 dead and 5 cars sunk in 20 s.

- Seeing it coming, bracing (src/brace.js; Sean): the dust storm, cold wave and tsunami (while the wave is in) and every tornado on the ground (twister.js, so Be The Disaster's too) have a Brace with near(x, z) = [metres until it reaches there, the nearest point of it] (SweepFront.nearest; a tornado: distance less twice its reach). Every 0.25 s a quarter of the people: a civilian in the street 12-260 m ahead (300 for a tornado) with a clear line of sight (2-6 points along the line, no live building over 4 m tall on any) panics away from it once (people.panic: run, or whatever lives.respond picks); every 1 s drivers who see it (8 m to 1.3x the range ahead): car.panic 30 from that point, a U-turn if they were heading at it. Within 12 m of a storm wall (not a tornado) anyone in the street or on a roof curls up in a ball (people.braceT, 3.5 s, refreshed; anim 13 in figures.js: down on the heels, folded over the knees, arms over the head; their own step is skipped while curled, in BRACE states only). When the wall reaches them (Brace.wall, once each): one in seven is flung along with it (15 m/s dust, 12 cold, 17 the wave; 8-15 up) and tumbles; the rest stay curled (the wave knocks them gently into the water instead). Roof people are reached by the dust and the cold too now. Tests (60 s each): about 950-1070 saw it and ran, 31-44 drivers turned for it (3-6 U-turns), up to 140-255 curled at once, 13-16% of those reached swept away; worst 4-step frame 29-43 ms.

- Solar flare and the nuclear plant (Sean: always): the flare's wave runs on past the town as far as the power plant if it is further, and when it passes the reactor, D.plantSurge() ('The solar flare has knocked out the cooling at the nuclear power plant!') starts a Meltdown (its overheating warning, then the blast and fallout), unless one is already on or the plant has gone. Be The Disaster's flare does the same when it strikes within its radius + 60 m of the reactor. Harbor, Metro, Lakeside: 12 of 12 flares set it off, 0-13 s in.

- Disaster TV as the end of the world (Sean; one station, not two): from the menu it opens on the whole globe ('DISASTER TV · LIVE', 'The end of the world, as it happens. First: <town>') and dives into a random town (globe.choose with a key); the very first town's card reads THE END OF THE WORLD / LIVE FROM <TOWN> (the radio's station ident plays; Sean may record a TV opening line). It stays in a town about 3 minutes (TOWN_T 170 s, then finishes what is on) and moves on, half the time back to a town it has hit before: leaving, it remembers that town's ruins (world.js rememberDamage, as the Sandbox does); arriving where there are ruins it puts them back, card BACK IN <TOWN>, construction crews already at work on the four nearest the middle (rebuild.js Site), and the responders (response.js, on but not answering) send more crews in the lulls; the tally counts the crews. Next disaster: drawn from the market (below), never one of the last three.
- TV prediction market: before each disaster (10 s, and before the first) three disasters with odds (5-90%, summing to 100) and payouts (x100/odds); the next one really is drawn at those odds. Tap one to call it (change it until it closes). Called right: 'You called it!' and help comes early: for villains, kaiju, worm, aliens and meteor showers the heroes come at once (Heroes with no hold, brief); for the rest the rescue services (responders' respond every second, rescue boats on) for that disaster. Wrong: 'Missed it: <name>'. CALLS right/total by the LIVE badge, kept on the device (zday_tvcalls).
- Fix: a sound played exactly at the camera (a TV camera being hit) projected to NaN and the AudioParam pan threw once sound was on, which would have stopped the game; Audio.place falls back to centre.

- Searchlight rings (Sean's night screenshot): each beam stopped at its target with its open end at 30% brightness, so a beam seen near end-on showed as hard bright rings. Now a beam carries on past what it lights (1.7x + 25 m) to where it meets the ground, fading to nothing along its length (pow(1 - v, 1.3)), fades out when looked at down its length (uniform uAx), has 12 rings of vertices (to bend with the world's curve), and is not drawn under 16 m long (only the pool); the pool of light sits where the beam meets the ground.
- Time of day (Sean: night to full day in one frame): game.update eases the phase it hands daylight.set (dayP): normal ticking is followed exactly, a jump (a new run, a round, a town, TV's clock wrapping) rolls round the short way at 0.035 of a day a second (midnight to morning: dark to light over about 3 s at dawn); a new mode starts at its own hour. Disaster TV has one continuous clock (CLOCK, a day and night every 12 minutes, carried from town to town).
- Disaster TV: a Change camera button (tvcam.change: the next live camera in turn, the director holding off its own cuts 30 s); the helicopter always above what it films (a high flyer: aimed and focused right on it, the town behind; level with Tyrant it showed only sky), a roof camera looking up at something over 40 m cuts to a helicopter; the heroes come 30 s into villains, kaiju, worm and aliens even uncalled ('The hero signal is lit'); a scoreboard top right: CALLS right/made and POINTS (a right call pays 100 x its payout: 10000 / odds%, so a 25% call pays 400), kept on the device (zday_tvcalls).

- Theme song and the house band (Sean's 'My Little Disaster', public/music/theme.mp3, 3:34, re-encoded 128 kbps stereo 3.6 MB):
  - Menus: Music mood 'theme' (looped) while the menu is up or the globe is picking (setMenu from showMenu and menuOff); in a town the game's moods take over (crossfade).
  - The band (src/band.js, W.band / game.band, built in worldReady, updated in the frame loop, reset with resetWorld, dispose on moveWorld): four figures from the villain rig in black with black hair and a devilock (black suit, chalk skin; singer with a mic stand, guitar and bass slung across, drummer at a red kit), 0.85 scale, animated to 172 bpm (headbanging, strumming, a fist up, leaps). Venue: on the stadium's pitch (Metro, Bayview: a 150 crowd); else the best park or square in town (a stage 14 m wide, a crowd of buildings/18, 18-70; trees and parked cars there cleared); else a big flat roof with the crowd kept clear of the stair hut (Harbor, 13). Stage: a black deck, truss, a banner (MY LITTLE DISASTER, dripping), amps, speaker stacks, four sweeping coloured lamps (faint by day). Audience: an InstancedMesh of townspeople (their own figure kit, archetype looks), 60% holding phones up (anim 6), a third bouncing, phone flashes. Fireworks every 1.2-4 s from behind the stage (five colours, a second-colour crackle, a soft boom near), flame jets along the front every two bars (whoomph).
  - Sound: Music.bandEl, a third looping player at the song's own clock (coming back you hear it further on), gain = 0.55 x max(near: 1 - smoothstep(110, 420 m from the stage), in view: 0.75 x (1 - smoothstep(200, 900))); the background tracks drop back to 20% while it is heard; paused after 4 s unheard.
  - It stops (Band.stop: the music cut in 0.08 s, the band gone from the stage, the crowd running off, a toast): the stage's building down (stadium or roof), a disaster's danger zone over it, the building or (park) a fire within 22 m burning, a pit under it, water over the stage, zombies within 35 m. Back on with the next reset of the world (a new run, round or town). Costs about 0.6 ms a frame headless.

- Searchlights rebuilt as light shafts (Sean: still bad at some angles): no cone any more; each beam is a flat strip (PlaneGeometry 1x1, 12 steps along) laid out in the vertex shader along the beam (uA lamp, uD direction, uL length, radius uR0 0.35 at the lamp to uR) and turned about its own length to face the camera (cross(dir, to-camera)), soft across (exp(-4.5 x^2)), fading along its length, fading when seen down its length (|dot(dir, view)| 0.7-0.96) and when the camera is within a few radii of it. No outline or open end to show from any angle.
- Quarantine rings (Sean: what are the yellow circles?): Commander's open streets out of an outbreak zone; each now has a police car badge in it (instanced, flat) so it says park a police car here.
- Zombie night (Sean: night for the whole zombie time, to see them glow): game.update sends the daylight to midnight (0.66) while an outbreak is on (8+ zombies or a toxic spill alive; a mode can say for itself with zombieNight(): Be The Disaster only for its zombie run, so the other runs' starting outbreaks do not bring the dark), always forward, through dusk and later dawn (about 17 s each way at 1x), back to the mode's own hour when it is over.

- Heroes against the alien invasion (src/herofight.js; Sean): with an invasion on (and not leaving), every free hero takes job 'alien' (after any meteor, missile or villain work): the nearest walking tripod nobody holds (a tripod preferred by 60 m), else the nearest saucer.
  - Tripod: flies to its first foot; heroesWin (66%) or it kicks them off ('The tripod kicked Nova away!', heroLost so nobody tries that one again). Grabbed: state 'held' (Tripod.update skips it; rays off, anyone held dropped), lifted up into the air on the end of its leg (32 m out) in 1 s, swung round, faster and faster, for 1.6 s (the held foot in the hand, the other legs trailing, the body tilted), then let go across the town toward the far side of the middle (at least 120 m, landing kept 50 m inside the town; vy 22): 'hurled', tumbling, legs flailing, smoke; it lands a wreck: a big burst, dust, a car fire, people crushed within 10 m, buildings within 12 m down.
  - Saucer: half the time straight through it at 95 m/s (it bursts, shootDown, spirals down burning and crashes as before; 'X flew straight through a saucer!'); else grabbed ('held'), swung round twice in 1.4 s, then flung up at 140 m/s and climbing, out of sight past 700 m: gone, counted as down (spaced), the invasion ends when every saucer is down or spaced.
  - herofight.orphans (each invasion step): a machine whose hero was knocked away or left mid-move falls and crumples (tripod) or gets away (saucer), so nothing hangs in the air. Tested at Northgate: heroes ended a full invasion in about 30 s.

- Globe road as a trail (Sean: only where you came from, never where you are going; the menu shows none until you travel): Globe.buildRoad(n) draws the road only from the first town to town n (rebuilt when n changes; n < 1 none) and showRoad(on, mode) uses globe.trail(mode) = profile.been(mode): the furthest town played in that mode (profile.played now records data.been[mode]; older saves: max of the last town played and the towns cleared). In Commander or Be The Disaster (the in-game globe, the road picker) that mode's trail; on the menu the furthest of either. A fresh save shows no lines.

- Back to the menu through the clouds (Sean: the way in, backwards): every Menu button (Commander, its end card, Be The Disaster, Sandbox, Disaster TV) and Be The Disaster's Disasters / Other disaster call game.toMenu(q): the mode goes quiet, sky.up lifts the camera straight up off the town into the clouds, then the menu page loads arriving inside them (Sky.leaving); there the clouds clear (as before) while globe.pullBack(the town you came from) starts the planet camera just over that town and pulls back over 2.4 s to the menu's view. Falls back to a plain page load if the sky is busy or there is no town.

- The Far Isles and Kiyomura (step 1 of Sean's plan for towns round the world, Asian architecture on the far side: modern Tokyo-style and traditional Japanese towns):
  - Globe (globe.js): ISLES, an arc of [lat, lon] points on the far side (lat 0.22 to 0.86, lon -1.5 to -2.05, east of the far continent); paint() pulls the height towards an island profile within the arc's half width (wider in the middle, two straits at t 0.14 and 0.74 make three islands, a strait all round), never desert there, a snowy cone (PEAK) on the main island, a speckle of cherry-blossom pink on its green. EAST = { kiyomura: [lat, lon] }: an eastern town is placed near its spot instead of in the band facing you. New towns go at the END of PLACES (placeTowns hands out spots in that order from one dice stream: inserting one earlier moves every town after it). The menu backdrop looks at the home towns only (the isles come round as it turns).
  - Kiyomura (places.js): 7 blocks, round, style 'japan', landmarks pagoda and temple, forest land; PATH now ends ... 'metro', 'kiyomura' (level 12). A save that already beat Metro wins it again to open Kiyomura (the old road could not open anything after it).
  - Building kit (city.js, opts.style 'japan', JAPAN): building() hands each lot to jHouses: narrow townhouses side by side (6 to 9 m), a little garden now and then inside a block (grass, a tree, a stone lantern). jHouse: walls in B.fac[4] (textures.js jfacade: 12 x 12 m from the pavement, a ground floor of red-brown lattice / paper doors under curtains / open shops, white plaster and timber above with slatted windows, paper screens and rails, lit at night), 1 to 3 storeys, a gable roof with its ridge along the street (or hip, 30%), eaves out 0.75 m, gable ends walled (gables()), a ridge cap and end tiles, a pent roof over the street at the ground floor's top. Roof tiles: a new bucket B.tile / mats.tile (textures.js tiles(): rows of round tiles, DoubleSide so the eaves show from below), in the snow cover list (weather.js). roofRings(rings, col, mask) builds any tiled roof from rings of eaves up to a ridge line or point.
  - Records: a pitched building's roof (where people stand, heroes land, fire burns) is a strip along the ridge at roofY = ridge height; rec.area is its floor, which people.js now uses for how many fit inside (b.area ?? the roof's area). frontDoor() is the street door code, shared with building().
  - Landmarks: 'temple' (jTemple: a stone terrace, a veranda of red posts and beams, white walls and paper screens, hallRoof: a curving hip roof, flat at the eaves and steeper to the ridge, gold ends; a red torii at the street, a paved path between stone lanterns, trees) and 'pagoda' (jPagoda: five red storeys, each smaller under its own wide roof, a bronze spire of rings). Gate, lanterns and the yard's trees are in the landmark's ranges and go down with it. addTree: half the crowns cherry pink in a japan town (a fifth of the outskirts' trees).
  - Fixed on the way: outskirts.js inst() drew one prop at the town's middle when its list was empty (a palm in every town with no beach): count = list length now.
  - Later steps: landmarks after more famous ones (a sky tower, a gate standing in the sea; never twin towers), 4 or 5 eastern towns in all, bot playtests.
- Hoshimachi, the neon city (step 2; places.js style 'tokyo', 9 blocks (10 was 680k vertices, 35% over Metro; 9 is 552k), EAST spot [0.4, -1.8] on the main island's south-west coast, after Kiyomura on the road):
  - city.js TOKYO: building() hands an uncut lot to tSlices (75% of lots over 11 m: narrow 'pencil' buildings 5 to 9 m wide side by side), floors 4 to 10 (12% low, tall% towers of 12 to 24) scaled up downtown, width cap 2.2 x the narrow side, modern facade styles 0/1/2.
  - Signs (tSigns, in a new bucket B.neon / mats.neon, in each building's ranges): tall boards standing out from the wall (a column of made-up characters, textures.js signBoards), banners across floors, a board on legs on 30% of roofs over 5 floors; the material adds its own colour as emissive (uNeon: 0.3 by day, 1.8 at night from daylight.js; the solar flare blackout dims it). None stand out over the station's platforms.
  - Scramble crossing (city.scramble): the crossroads nearest the first downtown with every street open; crosswalks always, diagonal stripes both ways (stripes can turn now: rot), crowds on its corners, big screens (the texture's three ad panels) on the buildings looking onto it; its four corner blocks are kept from landmarks.
  - Railway (city.rail laid out in city.js: a street from j 3, NB-3, 2... with no superblock across it, pillars at the thirds of each block clear of roundabouts, 70 m on past the town; src/rail.js builds it): instanced spans (deck, parapets, ballast, rails) and pillars, a station mid-town (platforms, canopy), two five-car trains (green, orange stripes; windows lit at night) shuttling end to end, stopping 7 s at the station and 9 s at the ends. city.hooks.street (called by takeStreetNear) breaks spans and topples pillars for R >= 5 while a heavy disaster is on (not weather, riots, zombies, floods, heroes); the kaiju breaks it walking through. A train short of a gap waits; one that cannot stop runs on and its cars fall off one by one (toast: A train ran off the broken line!). game.rail.step(h) in the sim step; reset with resetWorld.
  - Landmarks: 'lattice' (tLattice: a red lattice tower after Tokyo's, 104 m of curving braced legs in orange with white bands, in the glowing bucket so it shines orange at night, glass decks at 42% and 72%, a banded mast to 132 m, a glass building between its feet), 'spire', 'stadium'.

- The second theme song and the girl band (Sean's second My Little Disaster, kawaii metal, public/music/theme2.mp3, 3:27, re-encoded 128 kbps stereo): in the Far Isles' towns (a place with a style) band.js puts on four goth metal girls instead of the punks (mats(girls): black with pink; pigtails on each head, pink on black hair and black on pink, a pink tie each; pink leggings on the singer and bassist through the rig's new mt.leg (villains.js, boxy and close-up bodies); pink hair on the bassist and drummer; a pink guitar, pink drums, a pink banner), and Music.bandSong = 'theme2' (the band's player switches its source when the town changes).
- Menus: both theme songs one after the other (Music.menuSong; no loop: near the end of one it crossfades into the other, from update in a town and from the players' timeupdate on the menus, where the game loop does not run).
- PLAY screen (main.js): a fresh page on the menu (not one come back up through the clouds) first shows the title and a pulsing PLAY button over the planet, the menu card and button column hidden (body.zd-gate); the tap (or Enter) starts the sound and the music (audio.js first tap) and the menu pops up.
- Volumes (Sean): the stage's flame jets and fireworks a fifth as loud (gain 0.12, 0.07); the announcer much quieter (LEVELS 0, 0.18, 0.33, 0.55: loud is the old quiet) starting at quiet; the sound effects quieter (0, 0.2, 0.38, 0.6) starting at quiet; music starting at medium. New storage keys (zday_sound2, zday_music2, zday_radio2) so every player starts once at the new defaults.
- The pause button and the compass now sit at the top of the right-hand column (ui.js sideAdd, CSS order -2 and -1), above the sound button: every round button on one side.

- Roster: a section The bands, all eight (band.js bandMember / playPose, shared with the stage; bandModel: playing, the drummer at a little kit, the singer at a mic stand).
- Menus restyled (Sean): dark purple panels, neon gradient buttons with white writing (Commander cyan, Be the Disaster pink, Sandbox green, Disaster TV orange, the rest violet, main pink), the PLAY screen's logo at the top (Impact, Futura-CondensedExtraBold on iPhones) with the line Save it, wreck it, or sit back and bet on it! (on the PLAY screen too); the roster's plinths purple.

- Towns spread over the planet (Sean: one or two to each continent): globe.js SPOT gives every place a [lat, lon] to be placed near (EAST is gone): mesa and pines on the south-west continent, crossing and northgate in the north lands, dust and lakeside on the desert continent, harbor and sunvale on the big east continent, ironside in the south, bayview and metro on the capital's island; the Far East: sakurazaka and minato on the great continent by the Far Isles, takamori on its far side, kiyomura, umihama and hoshimachi on the Far Isles. The menu backdrop starts over where you are.
- Four more eastern towns (places.js; the road after Metro: sakurazaka, kiyomura, umihama, minato, takamori, hoshimachi): Sakurazaka (japan, 5 blocks, an ell, a pagoda: a hill village), Umihama (japan, 6, a crescent round its bay, a temple, coast), Minato (tokyo, 7, a long strip, a skyscraper, railway and scramble, coast), Takamori (tokyo, 8, twin downtowns, skyscraper and stadium).
- The band: in a village with no park of 22 m it takes one of 15 m; heard only looking right at the stage (near the middle of the view) with the point you look at within about a block (45 to 80 m) and the camera not far out (260 to 420 m).
- Menu music by the side of the world in view (Sean): globe.onFacing (every 0.5 s on the menu: the town nearest the middle of the view) sets Music.setMenuSong: a Far East town, theme2, else theme; crossfaded (4 s, not back and forth faster than every 3 s); each theme song picks up where it was paused (Music.pos), round again at its end.

- Menu planet (Sean: zoomed in on the pole; it should all show above the menu): the backdrop's camera placement had been commented out by a mid-line comment (fixed; today's changes scanned for more). Globe.menuFit: the camera far enough back (and the view shifted) for the whole planet to sit in the gap between the title and the menu card, measured from the page (a long panel leaving no gap: behind it all), eased to again when the card changes, left alone after so a pinch still zooms. The PLAY screen's logo and button sit low, under the planet.
- The call sign (You're listening to K-D-I-S, Disaster Radio) only once, on the menu 2.5 s after PLAY, far off as if heard from space over the planet (radio.farIdent: no words on screen, a lowpass at 1.7 kHz, mostly a 3.6 s echo, quiet); no more at the start of a game.
- Menu music far off too (Music.space: lowpass 2.6 kHz, dry 0.3, a 3.2 s echo at 0.5; the echo joined in only on the menus); close again in a town. Music.reverbIR makes the echo for both.

- Disaster TV cameras (Sean): the rooftop cameras are now viewers' phone videos (VIEWER VIDEO n, held by hand: a wobble and a tilt, never level; on air 8 to 13 s), cut to less often (from a helicopter 40% of cuts, else the other helicopter); pans are a spring (no snapping) leading a moving target by 0.6 s so it stays mid-screen; a wave or storm front is framed on the wall itself (game.front), the helicopter easing round to out ahead of it looking back; its distance eases too; now and then a slow zoom (camera fov to 55-70%, a phone 45-60% and more often) held 2.5 to 5 s and back, out at once if something comes for the camera; a cut puts it back; fov restored on leaving TV.
- Kaiju fire breath: act 'fire' (every 16 to 28 s of choosing): a head rears back for a breath (0.7 s) then lowers, jaws wide, and pours a jet of flame (two additive Splash emitters, outer and core, 40 m/s with the splash fall 4.9 t^2) along the way it faces, sweeping a little; where it lands (a roof or the street) fire bursts every 0.3 s, buildings within 9 m catch (55%), cars burn, people run; 40% of the time the other head takes a turn 1.2 s later. Toast: The monster breathes fire!

- The main menu is a flying saucer over the planet (Sean's picture): game.js SAUCER (an SVG: the underside with a dashed ring of lights, the rim edge on, the lighter metal hull with seams and lights, the dark deck, the dome on its glowing ring, the beam orb) under the real buttons (.box.saucer .ufo: the four modes as glossy neon panels on the deck, the outer two turned and tilted, the middle two leaning back; Units and Roster small on the rim either side of the orb); the level in a notched panel under it (.xp); body.zd-main floats the sound buttons out in space (top right, bobbing) instead of docking them in the card. The other panels keep the purple card.

- Killer plant (src/plant.js, hazard 'plant', Commander level 6; Sean): takes root on the nearest street (the heart: a dark red bulb with a crown of thorns, 100 hp); tips grow 3.3 m/s along the streets (instanced vine pieces, green with red veins, thinner as they branch: 72% at each crossing, up to 18 tips), wander across the road, climb walls beside them (a small building with three vines up it is crushed), flowers pop open along them; car-sized flytraps (flytrapModel: stalk, leaves, two spiked jaws green outside and red in) rise every 6 to 10 s (up to 14) and snap up a person (6.5 m) or a car (7 m), chew, gape again. Fire kills it (burnAt: pieces catch and fire runs along to their neighbours, flytraps lose hp, the heart 1.6 x power a call): vines on burning buildings, car fires, an airstrike's blast, a nuke, the kaiju's breath (D.burnPlants), a flame team (FlameTeam: an APC, four soldiers whose flamethrower jets torch the nearest of it within 32 m for 45 s), a napalm run (helis.napalm: an A-10 low along a line, fire bursting behind it, buildings catching). Heart burnt out (or 300 s): it all withers. Commander units Flame team (level 5) and Napalm jets (level 6); the Sandbox's responders send them (after 25 and 50 s); Disaster TV has it; the roster has the flytrap.

- Dimensional rift (src/rift.js, hazard 'rift', Commander level 9; Sean's picture): a standing oval ring (34 x 40 m) facing a random way: a swirling void (shader: black middle, blue and pink towards a white-hot edge), a crackling rim, a soft halo, lightning ribbons redrawn 12 times a second, a lightning strike about it every 4.5 to 8.5 s, cracks in the ground; the street torn up and drawn in; the twenty nearest buildings within 95 m bent out of true (city.warpBuilding: lean towards it, twist, wobble; restored when it closes). Orb beings (up to 6) drift out every 6 to 11 s and disintegrate cars, parked cars and buildings in a burst of light; tentacles (up to 3) reach out of the side facing their target for a building (pulled down, debris drawn in) or a person. It is shut by force (100 hp): heroes (job 'rift', riftStep: smash orbs, else beam into it from in front, 1.3 a s each), soldiers (shoot orbs within 75 m, 4 a soldier per 0.4 s, tracers; fire into it within 110 m), airstrikes (16 a blast within 75 m), a nuke; then it implodes in a flash; or it closes itself after 240 s. The Sandbox's responders send an APC squad and airstrikes (auto heroes count it); TV has it (heroes come); the roster has a little one.

- The menu saucer spins and its buttons float (Sean): the hull's panels, the lights round its edge, the underside strip and the dome's lights are dashed rings (.spin) whose dash offset runs (zdSpin, 2 to 7 s, a whole number of dash patterns so no jump), the far side going the other way; the four mode buttons drift and tilt a little each on its own clock (zdBob on the translate and rotate properties, so their own tilt stays); both off for reduced motion.

- Zooming in on the menu planet brings its songs closer (Sean): globe.onZoom (every 0.15 s on the menus) gives 1 at the menu's own view and 0 at 1.6 R; music.setZoom(k) -> applySpace: lowpass 2600 Hz to 20 kHz, dry 0.3 to 1, reverb 0.5 to 0 (only while space() is on; in a town it stays close).
- The saucer hums on the main menu (audio.ufo(on); showMenu/menuOff and main.js start it): two saws at 55 and 55.8 Hz beating plus a sub at 110 Hz, through a resonant lowpass (320 Hz, Q 7) swept by a 0.42 Hz wobble, and a faint 440 Hz whine with vibrato; gain 0.12 on the effects bus (Sean: much quieter than the first 0.45), so the sound setting sets it. Waits for the first tap (ufoWant).
- Fix: the menu planet's easing into its place never finished (the controls rounded the length off by more than 0.05), so it pulled every zoom back; it now ends within 1% or after 3 s, and any touch on the globe (controls 'start') stops it.
- Town names on the globe (Sean): none behind the menu (any panel) or while it turns and flies down to a town from it (state 'path': Commander, Be The Disaster, Disaster TV). Picking a town (Sandbox, and the world from a town) the label is the name only for towns won in (globe.known -> profile.beaten: data.beat[key], set on a Commander win of 2+ stars or a Be The Disaster goal; older saves: the towns either road has gone past) and the one you are in; the rest say ??? and still take you there. Commander/Be keep the road number, tick and lock around it (townStatus returns num, mark, locked, why).
- The killer plant grows out every way, more aggressive (Sean): tendrils (tips with axis 'v', plant.vein(x, z, a, r, gen)) wander off at any angle over pavements, lots and roads, thinning as they go and forking smaller ones (gen 0 to 3, 32/13/7/4 m x 1-2), and climb any wall they meet (MAXCLIMB 12 vines up walls at once). Eight leave the heart at the start, the street vines throw one off to the side 30% of pieces, and every 0.9-1.7 s a new one leaves the heart or a grown vine (up to 40). Faster (GROW 4.4), more street tips (26; a crossing branches 85%, both ways 40%), climbs 14%, flytraps every 4-7 s (up to 20) and reach a little further; a building of under 50 m with four vines up it is crushed. MAXSEG 3200 (the instance buffers go to the screen only while something grows or burns). Crossing test: 1370 pieces at 60 s (was 250), 10 buildings down, a flame team and napalm still burn it out in about 30 s.
- Sandbox's Disasters tab (Sean): A to Z (Toxic spill sorted in with the rest), each a card with a stylised picture over its name (src/thumbs.js: THUMB[kind], flat SVG scenes 96 x 54, each its own sky over a strip of town; gradient ids zt-<kind>). zombieLabel writes the count into the card's .nm so the picture stays. A new disaster wants a THUMB entry (no picture: an empty box).
- Bug and speed sweep over every mode (2026-10-28; scratchpad sweep.cjs, leak.cjs, tris.cjs, prof.cjs): menu, its panels, the town picker and flying in; Sandbox in the big city with all 21 disasters one after another; Commander for 4 minutes; Be The Disaster with each of its 21 kinds played with a finger; Disaster TV for 3 minutes. No page errors anywhere. Measure timings with nothing else running: swiftshader browsers side by side slowed each other ten times over and made Be look like it froze.
- Lighter frames (from the sweep: about 4.8 million triangles a frame in the big city's usual view): zoomed out past 300 m from the point in view, or flying, the tree crowns (city.lod: icosahedra of detail 0, 120 triangles instead of 480), the people (people.lod: figureGeometryLow, 108 instead of 236, the same tags and per-person attributes) and the cars in traffic and parked (traffic.lod: a body and a cabin block, the roof in the car's colour, 24 instead of ~400) go to fewer pieces; main.js switches them (city.far). The cars cast shadows from a block each (layer 1, the car's instance matrices, the count matched in onBeforeShadow) and the crowns from a detail-0 ball. Big city: 4.8 to 1.9 million triangles a frame; flying 4.9 to 2.0.
- The killer plant's flytraps are 8 meshes on shapes made once and shared (trapShapes, userData.shared) instead of 30 each (every tooth was its own mesh: up to 600 draw calls), and plant.freeModel frees a burnt flytrap and the whole plant on reset (it left ~120 shapes behind each time). Leak test (each disaster run twice, reset between): nothing grows the second time.
- Thrown whole objects (cars a storm, tornado, wave, black hole or blast picks up are each a mesh: one draw call, two with its shadow): debris keeps them to about OBJ_CAP 110 - past it each newcomer sends the oldest wreck lying still (else the oldest afloat) sinking away - and past OBJ_SHADOWS 60 newcomers cast no shadow. A dust storm in Northgate: 486 objects and 816 draw calls at 40 s before, about 200 and 125 after.
- Flying a plane in the Sandbox (Sean; src/flight.js): Disasters tab, Fly a plane: the stunt plane (red and white, its propeller turning, 22-72 m/s) or the fighter jet (the F-16 with an afterburner glow, 45-150 m/s). Chase view (camera 17/28 m behind, easing after it, no roll) or cockpit (in its seat, with a horizon line and a ring); Faster, Slower, Land. A finger anywhere is the stick (#zd-fly over the canvas, under the buttons): left and right bank it into a turn, up climbs, down dives; let go and it levels. Keys: arrows/WASD, Shift/Ctrl, V, Esc. Ceiling 650 m; 450 m out of town it swings back. Into a building or the ground: a fireball (fire.burst, the building set alight), back in the air at the edge of town 2.6 s later. Low over the streets people duck. While up: main.js hands it the camera (flight.frame after compassStep; no controls, no clamp to the town), fov 62/72, near 1.5/0.5, far 4000, no depth of field, see-through only in the chase view, the bottom bar, panel and world button hidden (body.zd-flying), the cheaper distant models on; audio.engine(kind, k) buzzes or roars with the throttle, low on the effects volume. Land restores the camera over where it was.
- The planes' payloads (Sean): the payload button goes round LOADS[kind] (stunt: storm seeding, small bomb, big bomb, napalm, nuke; jet: the bombs), Drop (or Space; Q the payload) lets one go, greyed for LOAD.wait. A bomb (helis.bombGeo) falls from under it with the plane's speed and lands on the ground or a roof: small (fire.blast + people.blast, 12 m), big (26 m, small buildings under 30 m within half of it collapse), napalm (the napalm run's bursts along its line, 6 x 8 m), nuke (helis.nukeBlast 200 m, flash and shake; within 120 m and under 300 m up it takes the plane too). Storm seeding: 5 s of flares behind it, then the weather a step wilder (clear to rain to thunderstorm; in a thunderstorm over the town a tornado comes down under it) - the opposite of Commander's cloud seeder, which calms storms. Up and down: down climbs and up dives, like a pilot's stick (keys the same way); Invert (or I) swaps it, kept in localStorage zday_fly_invert.
- On the ground in the Sandbox (Sean; src/drive.js): Disasters tab, On the ground: Tank or Police officer. Third person (behind, easing after) or first (eye height, the seat; then the officer, the car or the tank is not drawn). Left half of the screen: a stick from wherever the thumb lands (on foot it walks that way across the screen, up away from the camera; in a car or the tank up and down the throttle and reverse, the sides steer, the tank turning on the spot); right half: a drag turns the tank's turret or the officer's view. Buttons: Fire, Get in / Get out and Zombies (the officer), the view, Stop. Keys: WASD/arrows, J/L aim, Space, F, V, Esc. Walls stop it (it slides along them); 40 m past the town's edge it stops. The tank: shells at 150 m/s from the muzzle along the gun, bursting like a small airstrike (fire.blast 9 m + people.blast 6 m: the kaiju and the tripods feel it; a building under 22 m comes down 40% of hits, else its windows go), parked cars nearby burn up; it flattens parked cars and shoves traffic aside. The officer: three rides parked by the start (police car, fire truck, ambulance) and any police car or fire truck out on a call can be taken (traffic.remove, our own model in its place); getting out leaves it where it stands, on the driver's side. The pistol hits only zombies: the nearest within 45 m and about 18 degrees of the view, 90%, with a tracer and the shot's noise (zombies near come). Zombies: a tanker crash 60 m ahead (the toxic spill), every 15 s at most. main.js takes whichever of flight and drive is active (pilot: frame, focus, near, lod - 3: trees, people and cars all in fewer pieces). Engine: audio.engine('car'|'tank'). Big city: about 1.8 million triangles a frame driving or on foot.
- The disasters fight back against the plane (Sean; flight.js hazards, each frame it flies): the plane has a hull (100%, damage(n, why); smoke off it under 60%; at nothing it goes down). The readout (top, the numbers there hidden while flying or driving) has speed, height, hull and on a second line the nearest danger and how far (yellow when close, red when the hull is low). Tornado (twister): within 4x its funnel's width + 70 m at the plane's height (the funnel leaning with it) the plane is drawn in (50 m/s at full) and round (30) and shaken; within 1.5x + 4 m it is caught - spun round and up for 2.4 s, then torn apart. Over the top of the funnel (above its height x 1.15), only rough air. Seeding counts only under the cloud base: the run's closest pass (inside 160 m) takes 0.12-0.40 off its strength (closer, more), so two or three close runs; under 1.05 it ropes out. Kaiju (kaijuStep): within 48 m and under 80 m a warning, within 30 m and under 65 m a tentacle snatches it (2.5 a second); through the jet of its fire (in front, within 95 m) 32 a second; in range (260 m, under 190 m) a parked car near it hurled from 40 m up at where the plane will be, every 4.5-7.5 s (debris object; one within 7 m of the plane: 60). The bombs hurt it on top of the blast's 50: small 40, big 120, napalm 70 (the tank's shells 30) - 1200 to drive it off. Dust storm (devilStep): into a devil (under its height, inside its width there) 30 and a toss; a seeding run within 45 m of it, or a bomb within the blast's reach + 0.8x its width, takes it out (v.dead; fronts.js skips and hides it). traffic.geometryOf gives the whole car even zoomed out.
- More planes (Sean): the A-10 (gun, small and big bombs, napalm), the cloud seeder (seeding, small bombs), the B-2 (big bomb, nuke) and the B-52 (carpet bombs: a stick of 8, 0.16 s apart; big bomb, napalm, nuke) - the airstrikes' own models sized up (planeModel); the panel's Fly a plane has six cards (thumbs a10, seeder, b2, b52). Zoom: a pinch or the wheel (Z/X) - the chase camera's distance x0.45-3, in the cockpit how wide it sees. The fighters' gun (P.gun: F-16 40 rounds, 12 a second, 4 each; A-10 60, 14, 6): fires by itself at whatever is ahead (within about 9 degrees of the nose and its range): the kaiju (hp straight off, retreating at nothing), a saucer (hp, shootDown), a walker (damage), a dust devil (30); 75% hit; tracers; a magazine then 2.5 s reloading; the readout shows the rounds. Test: the A-10 took the kaiju 1100 to 616 in four passes; the F-16 shot down 4 of 5 saucers.
- Taking over a disaster in the Sandbox (Sean; src/takeover.js): Disasters tab, Be the disaster: Take over a unit. A label over each that can be driven (KINDS: a saucer or walker of an alien invasion, the worm, the kaiju, a tornado on the ground); tap one, then Take over. Third person, starting behind it the way it faces (K.face); left thumb moves it across the screen toward a point K.ahead off (the view swings round behind it slowly as it goes forward), a drag on the right turns the view, a pinch or the wheel zooms; keys WASD/arrows, Q/E the view, 1-3 its buttons, Esc. Buttons: saucer Beam up / Zap / Drop aliens (its own acts, started on the spot; another button changes act, the same again ends it; aliens.js: a taken saucer, s.player, chooses no act of its own), walker Beam / Heat ray (beamAt, fireAt), worm Breach! (camera above ground over where it tunnels), kaiju Throw car / Tentacle / Fire; the tornado just steers. The units' player switches (Be The Disaster's) stop their own thinking; Stop gives it back (the worm, kaiju and tornado then have a little while left). Lost (shot down, driven off, died out): back to the view from above with why.
- The main menu's buttons as Sean's picture (game.js WEDGE, wedgeBtn, FRAME, SIDES): four curved wedge panels side by side round the front of the saucer's deck, between an inner ellipse along the dome's ring (200, 86, 124 x 21) and an outer one along the rim (200, 120, 186 x 60), cut so each is about as wide on screen (outer x 22/112/200/288/378), 1.3 degrees apart. Each button covers its box over the hull (the same 400 x 230), its own SVG draws the wedge (a dark bezel, the colour's gradient, a neon edge with a glow, a lit line) and only the wedge takes the tap (the button has pointer-events none, the path visiblePainted); the label (icon and the name in capitals, no line under it - Sean) sits at the wedge's middle above the other panels, the outer two tilted 8 degrees with the deck. They sit still. A dark metal band (FRAME) holds them, round lights at either end of the deck and lit slots along the front of the rim (SIDES). Only the dome's lights and the underside's turn now; the middle of the ship is still.
- The menu saucer's orb is a fire button (Sean's Easter egg; game.js orbFire, menuShot): a tap fires a laser blast from it up onto the planet (globe.screenPoint, a point on the side facing the camera); held a second (it glows, then pulses red) and let go, a nuke there - a white flash, a fireball, a shockwave ring as wide as the planet (globe.screenRadius), a rising cloud, the nuke's sound. All in an SVG over the screen (#zd-menufx), gone after 3-4 s; nothing touches the map.
- Menu names (Sean): Defend, Destroy, Sandbox, Watch (Defend and Destroy were Command and Control until 2026-10-29; inside, the modes are still Commander and Be The Disaster). Colours (Sean, 2026-10-29): Defend green, Destroy red, Sandbox blue, Watch orange (Defend and Sandbox swapped). Each label (icon and name) turned along its panel's bottom edge (WEDGE lab[3]: the outer edge's slope on the 400 x 248 saucer, about 22 and 5 degrees).
- The orb's laser comes from a small gun under the saucer's belly (drawn in SAUCER at 200, 210-227); the beam runs behind the ship (#zd-menufx under the menu, z 5) to the planet, the muzzle flash and the nuke's white-out over everything (#zd-menuflash, z 8). Held 15 s (the orb pulses green: Sean) and let go: planetBuster - a beam like a pillar, the whole planet bursts (white-out, fireball, a shockwave three planets wide, 44 pieces flying) and is gone (globe.planetScale(0): everything in the globe's scene but the stars and lights shrunk away); 2 s later a black hole opens there (a dark well with a violet glow, a turning orange disk), draws the pieces back and the planet grows back out of it as it closes (6.3 s in all, then planetScale(1)).
- Controls on the ground and taking over (Sean, like Tornado Alley): the stick appears wherever the first finger comes down; a second finger drags the tank's gun or the view (taking over: sideways turns, up and down zooms). The officer faces the way he walks, the camera swinging round behind him, and his six-shooter fires by itself at a zombie within about 10 degrees of where he points and 40 m (a round each half second, 90%); after six it reloads itself in 3 s; no Fire button for him; the readout shows the rounds (filled and empty dots). Test: 9 zombies down in 10 shots with one reload.
- Sean's plan for the Sandbox (2026-10-28): spawn a disaster, then fight it - from a plane, a rescue vehicle or on foot (done: flight.js, drive.js), next as a hero, then the band's tap-the-notes mini game, and last controlling the disaster itself, more third person. His examples for the fighting: a tornado - flight paths and seeding without getting sucked in; a dust storm - flying into it to take out the small whirls inside; the kaiju - bombing runs with the jet while dodging the cars it throws at the plane and its tentacles. So the disasters should be able to catch the plane (and the cars, the officer) and be beaten by them.
- The menu's planet (Sean): a swipe sideways turns it and never tilts it (backdrop holds min and max polar angle at the tilt it has; choose() and open() free them again); one finger or the mouse dragged up zooms in, down out (globe.js pointer listener, state 'menu' only), as do a pinch and the wheel. The saucer's hum 0.09 (Sean: a little quieter). The Sandbox's town picker the same (choose without a key): the swipe only turns it and up/down zooms; its tilt eases (globe.tiltTo, in frame) from the menu's to the middle of the towns' latitudes (about 10 N), so all 17 can be turned into view. Turning to a town of the mode's own (Commander, Be, TV) and the world from a town stay free to tilt.
- Two mid-line comments had swallowed code (the patch pitfall above): the globe's controls had lost enablePan = false and their damping (since the menu zoom fix), and the kaiju had lost cars, grabbed and tentT = 3 (since its fire breath), so its tentacles never reached out and the car-throwing toast never came. Both split onto their own lines; a grep for code after a // found no others.
- A wider grep (any `name(...);` or `a.b = ...;` after a //) found three more, now fixed: the plane's seeding run never shrank a tornado or shortened its life (flight.js seeded: tw.applySize() and tw.life hidden), one kaiju head's tilt for pouring fire (kaiju.js), and a hotwired car kept the old car's colour and flee point (traffic.js). The check to run after every patch: `grep -rnoE " // [^'\"\`]*(\w\([^)]*\)\s*;|\w+(\.\w+)+ = [^;]+;)" src`.
- Bug battery (scratchpad battery.sh, every test one after another against a frozen build): menu, Sandbox, Commander, TV, Be The Disaster (all eight), flying, the six planes, payloads, the disasters fighting back, the officer's revolver, take over, the orb, the planet-buster, the globes' swipe and zoom, town names, leaks. No errors; no leaks (one shared shape the first spill makes stays, once).
- Be a hero (Sandbox; Sean: fly like the planes, lock on to catch villains, the kaiju game's mini games): Nova, Dragonfly and Tempo are flight.js kinds (PLANES nova/dragonfly/tempo with hero:, punch:, slow:). Flown with the same stick, slower (6-72 m/s), climbing as fast as diving, able to skim the street; into a wall they bounce off nose up (3 health) instead of crashing; knocked out at no health (a dust cloud, no fireball) and back up in a moment. The rig is heroes.js heroRig (heroModel now wraps it), lying along +x, posed each frame (heroPose: flat out with a fist ahead at speed, upright treading air when slow, a punch, arms out spinning a villain), placed by the hips (HIP). Choosing a hero brings Blur, Tyrant and Weaver to the middle of town if none are about. The bar: Lock on (Space) in place of the payload and Drop.
- Lock on: the nearest villain; the hero flies itself straight at them (homeIn: faster the further, over roofs in the way, through everything in the way: see Heroes after launch) and within 6.5 m the fight starts (heroduel.js Duel, an overlay over everything): WEAK SPOTS - six glowing circles over the villain, 0.45 s apart, each there 1.05 s (Tempo 1.52 s: time slowed); a tap lands a blow (26 x the hero's punch: Nova 1.4, Dragonfly 1, Tempo 0.9; Blur takes double, frail), a miss costs 7 health. Then the villain is dazed: SPIN & TOSS within 4 s, then circle a finger round the middle of the screen 2.5 turns in 5 s - the villain whirls round the hero, who rises to 14 m - and they are hurled away from the camera (toss, 80 x punch); too slow and they break free (10 health). After a throw, FLY AT THEM locks on again and the next round starts where they landed. v.heroWin is set for the player's fight, so a villain brought to nothing is beaten (the AI heroes' odds do not apply). About three rounds for Nova on Tyrant. The camera in a fight: over the hero's shoulder, 13 m back, 7 to the side, 7 up (clear of trees), held still through the spin. Villains close to a flying hero land a blow now and then (villainStep, 6 health); the readout names the nearest, the lock, or the fight. Landing mid-fight drops the villain. Sound: audio.engine('wind'), rushing air with the speed.
- The planet-buster's music (Sean): at the blast the song stops dead and its echo rings on for about a second (music.js blast: the dry cut, the wet echo turned up to 1.7 and dying away, the player paused 60 ms later); once the planet is whole again (5.6 s after letting go) the song comes back where it stopped, easing in (resume). The menu song does not change while it is stopped; music off, nothing happens to it. (A first try played the two seconds before the blast backwards as the black hole opened, taped with a ScriptProcessor; Sean did not like how it sounded, so it is gone.)
- Hero fights, second round (Sean: the villains rotated round all weird; a random move button; the hands at the feet in the spin). A held villain used the thrown-through-the-air tumble (body spinning at 9 rad/s); now f.pinned picks a pose of its own (villains.js pinPose, in all three villains' and the heroes' sync before the tumble): 'reel' facing the hero and flinching at each blow (f.flinch), 'dazed', 'swing' (stretched out flat, held by the ankles), 'air' (flailing, launched), 'drag' (on their back, hands at the throat; brawl.js drag sets it, so the AI heroes' and villains' drags no longer tumble either). toss() clears it. The spin: the hero turned square to the villain every step, arms straight out and in together (-1.5, -sx*0.24), hands 1.24 m ahead of the hips and 1.10 up (heroduel.js HANDS), the villain's feet put exactly there (measured 0.00 m apart). Dazed now shows SPIN & TOSS and RANDOM MOVE (never the same move twice running; by the neck only with them on the street): FLYING KICK (lunge, a foot in the chest, 45), LASER BLAST (Nova's from her eyes, the others' from their hands, each in their colour, 1.3 s pushing them back, about 60), BY THE NECK (brawl.js drag with a stand-in holder for the hero, through what is in the way, flung at the end, 35 + 25), SKY SLAM: hold a circle (2 s to full) to launch them 25-150 m, tap a circle to fly up past them, hold a circle (1.6 s to full) to smash them down at 40-120 m/s, the hero diving after; the crater as big as the hit (ring 18-70 m, dust, cracks, windows, cars flung, people bowled over not hurt, small buildings down at full power), 40 + 110 x power + a quarter of the height; they lie in it 2.4 s. Left alone the circles go off by themselves after 5 s; not tapped, they fall back on their own. The hero's poses for each (flight.js heroPose, F.act). Every move ends at FLY AT THEM. The camera through the slam watches the villain and keeps up with the hero.
- Play the band (Sean: click the band, hit Control, play the song; tap balls to the beat): rhythm.js BandGame. In the Sandbox a tap on the stage offers "Control the band" (game.js bindInput, before a person's card); the band is also in Take over's picks (takeover.js KINDS.band, the button reads Control the band) and has its own card (The band, THUMB.band). main.js hands it the camera like the planes (pilot list): square on to the stage, a 50-degree lens (the town's is 18) far enough back for the guitars either side, the rest of the bar hidden (zd-flying). The song's beat was measured from the files (band.js SONGS: theme 109.35 bpm, first beat 0.23 s; theme2 75.21 bpm, 0.138 s) and the band now moves on it (this.bps; theme2 at double, half-time). The clock is the band player's currentTime (music.js bandEl), smoothed, snapped after a slow frame, a new set if it loops; taps judged at the moment they happen (now(), between frames). Balls over the singer, the two guitars and the drums, a ring closing in over 1.2 s; PERFECT within 0.1 s (100), GOOD within 0.2 (50), x2/x3/x4 with the combo every 10; a miss resets it. The chart: the punk song on every other beat with more off-beats as the minute goes on, the girls' song on most beats with off-beats too; the drums on the bar's downbeats as often as not, never the same one three times running. A four-beat count-in, a 60 s set, then a grade (S 95%, A 85, B 70, C 50, D), the score and the best (localStorage zday_band_best). A hit makes that one leap (band.js mb.hop) and sparks off them; every 10 in a row the flame jets, every 25 fireworks. While playing the band is loud and the game's music right down (music.bandLoud). Music off: the balls keep the same beat silently, with a hint. The show broken up mid-set ends it.
- Pre-release review (2026-10-29; Sean asked for several lower-model agents: code review, modes, Sandbox, performance). Fixed from the code review: a fourth line hidden behind a mid-line comment (people.js: a screamer zombie's first-scream timer inc[i] was never set; the better scan, which catches bracket targets and comments with apostrophes: `grep -nE "[;)}] +// .*(\w+(\.\w+|\[[^]]*\])+ ?[-+*/|&]?= ?[^= ]|\w+\([^)]*\) ?;)" src/*.js`, clean now); the band game hid the town's bars only if a plane had been flown first (the zd-flying rule now in rhythm.js too); the band's and the fight's overlays sat over the side buttons (pause, sound, music untappable): now z-index 29 under them, and spots, hold circles and balls kept out of that column; a stage tap with a disaster picked and waiting no longer offers the band (mode.busy()); knocked out by the last missed spot no longer leaves the villain dazed for good (_spots and daze check this.over); a fight that ends without a win lets go of the lock (a villain breaking free was caught again the next frame); a set too near the song's end starts the song from the top (its loop would have started the set again); GO! after Stop no longer throws; Tempo's long spots no longer slow the pop and miss; the spin goes the way the finger goes (spinA -= d); no villains left: three more come (the old tip pointed at a panel hidden while flying); the hurt timer t2 runs every frame (no tips or sounds in a fight before); a hero is thrown, not 'your plane'; the hero's smooth body freed on Land. WebKit (Playwright's, Safari's engine, iPhone user agent): menu, Sandbox, a hero fight and a random move, the band game, a plane, Commander, Be The Disaster all ran with no errors. Screenshots for the store run on the GPU (headless shell with --use-angle=d3d11 sees the RTX 3070 Ti), far faster than swiftshader.
- From the performance agent (2026-10-29), done before release: the pixel ratio now follows the real frame rate (main.js: under 26 fps for 3 s steps down a quarter to 0.8 at the least, back up after 20 s over 45; measured in real time, since the frame step is capped at 0.05 s and flattered a slow phone; tested held to 15 fps: 1.5, 1.25, 1.0, 0.8 in about 10 s); the blur skips pixels in the sharp band with nothing blurred within its reach (8 depth taps instead of about 110 colour and depth taps; looks the same, frames about 23% quicker at full detail); the menu saucer's running lights tick round (steps(24), the SVG was repainted every frame: about twice the menu's frame rate); index.html shows MY LITTLE DISASTER / LOADING... until the first frame, and a saucer icon for the tab; the kaiju frees its mouth, teeth and eyes (17 shapes a run before). Its other findings, for after the launch: far people are 9 boxes each and every person and car is drawn even off screen (people.js mesh frustumCulled false; at the default view 4% of people are in view); the shadow map is redrawn whenever the camera moves at all; the PLAY tap starts about 10 MB of music downloading (music.js prime); backdrop-filter blur on the HUD buttons (hard on old iOS); window.__z keeps all of three.js in the bundle; the roster opens a second WebGL context; the spill, rift and worm free a few shapes short.
- From the modes play-test agent (menu, Commander, all 21 Be The Disaster kinds, Disaster TV, Units/Roster, at 390x760, 360x640, 1280x720, landscape and 320 wide): no crashes, no page errors, nothing stuck. Fixed: the incident banner went under the top bar when the alert pill grew and wrapped it (alertFlash places it again at 60, 400 and 1200 ms); the TV ticker's 'The rioters has spotted' (plural subjects take are/have); the saucer's Units and Roster labels get a bigger tap area (::after); the fps readout only with ?fps; on a phone on its side the menu saucer is smaller and the XP bar and footer are left off so it all fits. Left as they are: the Control picker's unlock line talks of Commander levels (the XP is shared); no Back during the flight down to a town; at 320 px wide a menu title overflows; the hollow stars on the Be end card are faint; going back to the menu reloads the page (slow on a weak phone).
- From the Sandbox play-test agent (all 21 disasters, weather, travel, the 6 planes and their hit-backs, tank, officer, take over, the heroes and every move, the band game, mixes of them, real touch events, 5 cycles of each for leaks): no page or console errors, no leaks. Fixed: the pause button now pauses the band game (rhythm.js holds it; music.holdBand pauses the band's song and moves its clock on so it goes on from where it stopped; nothing judged while paused); the song jumping mid-set (it loops, the music button turned off and on) no longer starts the set again (resync: the balls in the air dropped without misses, the beats laid again from there, score, combo and time left kept); leaving the mode (travel, the menu) puts away a plane, a drive, a take-over, the band game, its offer and Take over's pick (game.js endMode; a pick left up used to start an invisible unit in the next town); a Land button in a hero fight (the plane's bar is hidden while one is on); Esc stops the band game and lets go of Take over's pick; the radio's subtitle sits above the pilots' buttons (its bottom used the town bar's height, 0 while that is away); the band's results card is clear of the side buttons; Take over with no disaster on brings the band into view and says to start one; main.js asks for the next frame first and catches an error in a frame, so one failing part no longer freezes the game (tested with a thrown error).

## itch.io package (2026-10-29, for Sean's upload)
- The `itch/` folder: `my-little-disaster-web.zip` (the build, index.html at the top, 19 MB, not in git), `cover.png` 630x500 (the tornado past the band's stage, the logo along the foot), `logo.png` 960x400 transparent, `banner.png` 960x200, `background.png` 1920x1080, `screenshots/01-11` (8 desktop at 1280x720 full detail, 3 phone at 780x1520), `DESCRIPTION.md` and `description.html` (the four modes, the Sandbox's planes, heroes, ground, take over and band, the living city, controls, notes; the last line wants Sean's name), `ITCH-SETUP.md` (every field and the theme colours). The art is drawn as SVG in the game's look and rendered in the headless browser (scratchpad itch/make-art.cjs, make-cover.cjs); the screenshots are taken on the GPU (itch/shots.cjs, cover-shot.cjs). The skyline never has two tall towers alike side by side, and no plane is near a building.
- The zip was checked the way itch serves it (in an iframe under /html/<id>/): menu, Sandbox, the band game, a hero fight, Commander, saving, no missing files. Zip it with Windows' tar from inside the build folder (`tar -a -cf ../my-little-disaster-web.zip *`): PowerShell 5's Compress-Archive writes backslashes into the paths.
- itch's game area (Sean, 2026-10-29: the cover repeated behind Run game): itch draws the theme's Embed BG behind the button with `background-repeat: repeat` at the picture's own size (top left on a computer, centred on a phone, where the area is a strip about 390 x 80-160 under the button). `itch/embed-bg.png` is made at exactly 1280 x 720 (scratchpad itch/make-embed.cjs: the tornado shot, the middle clear for the button, the logo and tagline under it). Autostart (ticked) shows the game's own title screen on a computer instead; phones always get itch's launch button and full screen (itch docs).
- No zooming on a phone (Sean: two fast taps in the band game zoomed the whole page in and it wouldn't zoom back out). iPhone Safari zooms on a double tap whatever the viewport tag and touch-action say. nozoom.js (called first thing in main.js): of two taps under 450 ms apart (a tap: one finger, moved under 14 px, under 0.5 s), the second's touchend is cancelled, which stops the zoom, and its click is sent by hand (el.click()) so a button tapped twice works twice; drags, pinches and form fields are left alone. The band's balls judge on pointerdown, which comes first, so they still count (tested: 22 of 22 on the beat, 2 of them cancelled). If the page gets zoomed anyway, visualViewport's resize sees scale > 1.05 and rewrites the viewport tag, which sets it back (headless Chromium won't zoom, so this backup is untested; needs a look on the iPhone).

## Heroes after launch (Sean, 2026-11-01: the heroes are the best part, the planes next; one idea at a time)
- Through buildings (Sean: fly through buildings and they crash down or explode). flight.js plough(): a hero no longer bounces off walls (the old 'You bumped into a building!', 3 damage). Every frame the way from the last position is checked every 2 m (a fast hero on a slow phone can't skip a small building): a building whose footprint the hero is within 0.6 m of, below its roof + 0.8, is the one they are in (this.inB). Going in (burstIn; the bits kept under about a metre, as big ones flying past the close chase camera filled a phone screen): 18 bits of wall, glass and stone thrown back out of the hole toward the camera, a small dust cloud, crash and glass sounds, speed x0.85, a punch pose, a shake, people nearby scared. Coming out (burstOut): the far windows blown out (D.blowWindows), 22 bits ahead of the hero, then the building comes down (destroyBuilding 'collapse', leaning the way the hero went: dir atan2(-fx, fz), spare: everyone inside gets out first, noCrush). Under 30 m tall, 4 in 10 blow up first: 6 fireballs through the building, 24 bits flung every way, boomBig, a big shake. 30 m and up never blow up, they only fall in their dust (no fireball in a tower: Sean's no 9/11 rule). Landmarks fall too; the band's building coming down ends its show as before. No damage to the hero. Leaving a building mid-way (the fight starting inside one) brings it down at once; a crash or a respawn forgets it.
- Lock on goes straight through everything in the way too (Sean, 2026-11-01: it was going up and over, and should smash through just as when flown by hand); one lock-on across the big city can bring down a few dozen buildings, which is the point. Free flight tested in the big city: eight low runs, 96 buildings down, 33 of 89 low ones blown up, 0 of 7 towers, health untouched, no errors. The starting tip says so; the itch description too.
- Floors inside the buildings a hero smashes into (HERO-PLAN.md item 1, Sean's question: floors only once you smash in, not in every building). interior.js Interior: at burstIn the building is lifted out of the city mesh into its own copy (city.liftBuilding: extractBuilding plus hideBuilding(k, keep), which moves its vertices away but leaves it alive, so plough still sees it and disasters.lifted stands in for it) and given an inside: a floor slab every storey (b.fl storeys over b.roofY - b.baseY) as a grid of 3 m tiles inset 0.4 m, columns every 6 m the full height, furniture per floor (one box per 30 square metres, 50 on a weak phone: desks, shelves and cabinets in the greys and blues of a block over 30 m, beds, tables, wardrobes and chairs in browns and creams below), each kind one InstancedMesh, and a dark disc (r 1.1) at the hole they came in by. The materials glow a little of their own (emissive 0.28) because the sun never reaches under a roof. The shell's wall materials are swapped for back-face copies (cloned once per city material, the shader hook carried over): a doll's house cutaway, the near wall gone from outside, the far walls the backdrop, so the chase camera needs no moving. Every 2 m step inside (Interior.step) tiles within 1.9 m at the hero's height (within 1.3 m) are zeroed and drop three bits; furniture within reach is zeroed and flung ahead in its colour; columns stand. burstOut hands the group to destroyBuilding as opts.shell (disasters.js also takes disasters.lifted for a building anything else brings down, and removes it on a vanish), so the floors fall with the walls and debris.drop frees the geometry. dropInside() (a crash, Land, a respawn) brings a building the hero never came out of down. Landmarks get no inside (odd shapes), only the cutaway. Tested: 12 buildings in a row, every inside built (about 80 tiles, 33 pieces) and gone after the fall, tiles and furniture punched at a floor level, no errors, no health lost.
- The hero lab (HERO-PLAN.md item 2): lab.js LAB, one shared record (localStorage zday_lab): the hero's level and the villains' level (1 to 20), which moves are on (spin, kick, laser, drag, slam), and a readout. Levels: hero health 100 + 10 a level (flight.hullMax; the HUD's health is a percentage of it), hero hits x(1 + 0.06 a level) (heroduel pow); villain health 780 x(1 + 0.1 a level) (villains.js Fighter), villain hits x(1 + 0.05 a level) (the three villain hits on the hero: villainStep's 6, a missed spot's 7, breaking free's 10), weak spots last x(1 - 0.015 a level). The readout (LAB.stats, reset at take-off, shown as a third HUD line when Readout is on): damage dealt and taken, fights won/fought, buildings the hero brought down, people scared (people.scare now returns how many ran), and fear = scared + 20 a building + 50 a person hurt (hurt is not counted yet: the collateral meter, item 5, will). The Sandbox panel's Hero lab block (game.js, under Be a hero): two sliders, the five move buttons (a move off is left out of Random Move; spin off leaves only Random Move), Spawn Blur / Tyrant / Weaver / All three at the camera's target (disasters.start('villains', x, z, { only })), Readout on/off. Levels take hold at the next take-off or spawn. Outside the Sandbox the levels sit at 1 and every move is on, so nothing else changes.
- The bench: scratchpad lab-fights.cjs plays every hero against every villain at levels 1, 10 and 20 (sets zday_lab, reloads, spawns one villain, locks on, taps spots as they come, alternates the moves, spins the spin, holds and taps the slam, presses FLY AT THEM) and prints a line a fight (won, time, taken, dealt, knock-downs, buildings) and a summary a level. First run: 27 of 27 won, 19 / 23 / 25 s on average at levels 1 / 10 / 20, 0 damage taken (the script taps every spot at once: item 3 is where villains start landing hits), no errors. Run it after any change to the fights.
- Fights in the town's view (HERO-PLAN item 3a; Sean: the models are too plain up close for first or third person). flight.js frame(): while a duel is on, the camera is the town's lens (FOV_ISO 18) ISO_DIST 64 m (x the pinch zoom) away and ISO_EL 0.84 rad up, on the line from the villain through the hero (D.cam, set once a fight, so the hero is nearer the camera), aimed between the two of them, eased at 4 a second (12 in a sky slam, which it follows up), the blur on (post.opts.dof). duelOver puts the chase (or cockpit) lens back and the blur off; the chase camera eases back from there. The weak spots and circles were already screen overlays round the villain's projected position, so they work at this distance. Flying between fights is still the chase view (the plan's option of the town view with the hero as a dot is untried).
- Sean's inspiration (2026-11-01): a film's street brawl (hero vs two invading soldiers, the army attacking everyone). The beats are listed in HERO-PLAN.md item 3 (two villains at once, the hero thrown through buildings too, a rogue army unit, saving soldiers turns the army and lowers fear, a breakable mask that dazes a villain, villains retreat instead of dying). Taken as inspiration only: our own villains, names and places.
- Villains fight back (HERO-PLAN item 3b). heroduel.js: during the weak spots the villain winds up every windGap() seconds ((2.6 - 0.06 a villain level) x 0.8 to 1.3, x0.7 after a second wind): a flash and a whoosh, and a red dodge circle with an arrow to one side of them (.dodge) for 0.55 s x spotLife x Tempo's slow; tapped in time (dodged) the hero sidesteps 1.4 m that way and the swing misses; too late (dodgeStep) and it lands: villainHit(9), the spots up at the time lost (no extra damage), a crouch, a shake.
