Health counts now (sweep): a punch 1 (2 for the dazing ones and the dash blow, 3 for heavy, times the fighter's weight), kick 3, wave 4, burst 3, drag 3, the spin's swing 2, the signature 2 a blow; what is left standing staggers back a step with a spark (stagger). Robot brutes and troopers, and silver commanders, take more than one now too.
- Spitters (figures.js had the type, nothing made it spit): from 6 to 18 m one stops and lobs green goo at where she is heading, a green ring where it will land, 0.95 s; caught, SPAT ON! for 1.6 of a bite. Up to 4 in the air.
- Barrels: ten red barrels with hazard bands on the streets round the start (not in the giant fights). Any sweep near one sets it off (a punch within 2 m, or in front of her within its reach; bursts, waves, dashes, drags, landings): a 7 m blast, 4 off everything in it, cars thrown, the ones near go off a beat later. GRAB by one (within 1.6 m, or nearer than any zombie, measured also from where a drawn stroke's dash began in the last 1.2 s) carries it overhead; let go and it goes off where it lands.
- Grab moves, both fights: corner = SUPLEX (over her head into the ground behind her), out and back = SLAM (lifted high, down in front). Street fight: 20 and 17 (times weight and run), the foe bounced, a crack ring. Crowd: the one held dusted, a 3.6 or 4 m burst where it lands. THROW in the street fight now aims at the nearest standing building within 35 m and 70 degrees of where she faces, INTO THE WALL! (the building comes down and the foe takes its hit).
- Score (street fights): every bit of damage she does is worth 10 a point, plus 10% for each hit in the run (to 20); on the hit counter, HITS and the score.
- Test: brief-test.cjs (the mix, a juggernaut, a bloater, a spitter, a barrel punched, one carried and thrown, the crowd's suplex and slam, the street's suplex, slam, throw into a wall, the score).

## Destroy's chasers (Sean, HERO-PLAN item: jets and heroes chasing you, with mini games to shake them; 2026-11-05; chase.js)
- Made with every Destroy flying level (herogame start, !hero), stepped from update while she is out (paused in a street fight, the lair, on the way to the door), gone at finish.
- Heat: buildings down since the last chase against a quarter of the level's need (at least 5); the box at the top says HEAT n%. At 100% and 15 s after the last (20 s at the start), they come, by turns: jets, then a hero (one of those still heroes; none left, jets).
- JETS: two A-10s (1.15 scale) just behind her shoulders. Every 5 s a MISSILE LOCK: a red ring closes from 280 px to 40 px on a 96 px green mark over 1.3 s; a tap while it is 0.78 to 1.25 of the mark (it turns green) is a barrel roll, the bolt flies on and bursts in the town ahead; early, late or none, it hits for 14. Three dodged: THEY BROKE OFF! (+500). Under 22 m for 4 s: LOST THEM IN THE STREETS! (+600).
- A HERO: flies up behind her (flat, arms ahead), 2.5 s, then grabs: three arrows one at a time (left, right or up: never down, the tablet's full-screen swipe), 1.3 s each to swipe that way. All three: JUKED!, they swerve into the nearest building and bring it down, SHAKEN OFF! (+600). A wrong one or too slow: caught for 16, they drop back 4 s and come again.
- Points go on Destroy's score (StormWar) where there is one.
- Test: chase-test.cjs (three locks dodged, a hero juked, one lock left alone, lost low).

## The earthquake level's boss, options (Sean, 2026-11-05; golems.html, golemkit.js)
- Sean's design: a giant of rock with lava in its cracks, as big as a big building. It quakes and throws big stones; throw a few back and it falls, little stone men come out of the ground to be beaten, then it splits into two half its size, which do the same; those split into four a quarter the size, fought hand to hand; each of those breaks into a crowd that goes down in one hit.
- golemkit.js golem(design): a figure 10 units tall on the ground with joints to pose (hips, torso, head, arms sh/el/fist, legs hip/knee/foot) and tick(t) for the idle and the lava's glow. Rough stones are icosahedra with their corners pushed in and out by a hash of the corner (stoneGeo), flat shaded; lava is a bright basic material showing through the gaps.
- Four looks: A Basalt Brute (hunched, dark basalt plates, long arms), B Magma Core (boulders round a glowing core, lava at the joints), C Obsidian Titan (tall, black glassy crystals, a lava fault line, horns), D Living Mountain (a hill with grass, trees, a lamp post and a car on its back).
- Building heights measured: most 10 to 16 m; the tallest in a small town 16 to 34 m; Metro City has towers of 123 and 148 m. Options shown: 30, 50 and 80 m, beside 13 and 34 m buildings and the hero. The split card: 50, 2 x 25, 4 x 12.5, a crowd at 2 m.
- Waiting on Sean's pick before the level is built.

## The earthquake level: the Faultline (Sean, 2026-11-05: looks A and D, A in Asian towns and D in western ones; faultline.js, herogame.js quakeLevel)
- A quake on the story's board is now this level (herogame start: wave quake), fought on foot in the crowd fight (horde o.quake, ground: true; this.fl). Size 50 m (Sean did not pick one; the options page showed 30, 50, 80). The Basalt Brute in the Far Isles (towns with a style: japan, tokyo), the Living Mountain elsewhere (golemkit.js).
- Stage 1, one 50 m giant (rises out of the ground 70 m off): walks at 4.5 m/s to 45 m from her, through any building in its way (trample). By turns: STOMP (a leg up 0.9 s, down: a ring of broken ground rolls out at 22 m/s to 75 m; on the ground where it passes, 2.2 bites; dashing, gliding, in the air or mid-signature it passes under her) and THROW (an arm back 0.85 s, a boulder 2 m across lobbed 1.3 s at where she is going, a red ring where it lands; under it, 1.8 bites; it bursts what is round it). In the air she is thrown at too (12).
- A landed boulder stays 16 s (a yellow ring, at most 4): GRAB by one (within 2.6 m of its edge, or of where a drawn stroke's dash began) carries it; let go and it flies at the nearest big one standing, homing in (48 m/s): DIRECT HIT n/3, it staggers. Three: IT'S GOING DOWN, it topples back (1.5 s), lands (what it lands on comes down), sinks, and 24 stone men come up out of the ground round it (a new type, figures.js stoneman: grey stone, no glow, one hit). All dust: IT SPLITS IN TWO, two of 25 m rise there. They do the same, two hits each, 12 stone men each, then AND AGAIN: four of 12.5 m.
- Stage 3, the four small ones: no quakes, no throws; they walk up and pound the ground where she stands (a red ring 0.8 s first; 1.6 bites), and take her blows (10 health, more with danger; a punch 1, heavy 3, as the crowd's). Punching a big one only sparks: TOO BIG TO PUNCH, THROW ITS BOULDERS BACK. Each small one down crumbles into 10 stone men; the last of them dust and the town is saved.
- The count waits for it (giantsLeft: giants standing and splits to come). Stone men take the town's spare people (140 hidden at the start) and then the places of stone men already dust (spent), so every wave comes up full.
- The camera (horde place): a giant near is framed as the worm and kaiju are, and for the Faultline lower (0.24 against the usual angle), further (55 + 0.8 of its size) and wider (40 degrees on a phone held upright, 34 for the small ones), aimed at 45% of its height, eased back after.
- Test: quake-test.cjs (it rises, a quake ring, a boulder grabbed with a stroke and thrown back with a swipe for a direct hit, the fall, the stone men, both splits, a small one punched with taps from 10 to 4, the crowd, TOWN SAVED), western and Asian towns.

## Watch: betting on the heroes and the disasters (Sean's plan item 9, 2026-11-05; tv.js, fightbot.js)
- Superseded in part the same day (next section): Showdown and the old Sandbox disasters are gone from Watch; each town gets one disaster the way it is in Defend.
- Coins on the device (zday_tvcalls: coins from 1000, the stake, calls right / made). Every slip has a STAKE row (50, 100, 250, ALL IN); the stake comes off when the slip closes and a right call pays it back x100/odds. Under 10 coins at a slip, the station spots you 200. The scoreboard under the pause button: CALLS and COINS (hidden in a fight); the coins also beside STAKE on the slip. While a bet rides, a pill above the ticker: YOUR BET: n on X, pays m.
- The programme goes round What hits next, Fight Night, What hits next, Showdown (SEQ, carried from town to town). Each slip is open 12 s.
- What hits next: as before (three disasters at odds, drawn at them, help comes early when called right), now without the villains, the kaiju and the worm, which have their Showdown.
- Fight Night: one of the hero side against one of the villain side (HS.team, the viewer's own names and outfits, their levels on the bars), in the street fight at a walkable spot near the middle (fighttest ring r 26, street). The odds: 50% + 7 a level between them, give or take 13 for the night's form, kept within 22 to 78. The villain side has 400 health and 10 a level (the bot hits faster than a thumb). While the slip is open the two stand 4 m apart facing each other on the fight camera, above the slip (o.watch.wait). Then the winner is drawn at those odds and the fight is played to it: nobody at the controls, a bot presses what a player would for the hero side (fightbot.js: walks in, taps the combo, blocks or dashes at the last moment of a wind-up with a chance that grows with her level, counters, mashes free of a grab, the two powers, GRAB for suplex, slam, throw or drag, TEAM when full, a finisher at zero, scripted ones by choice), the foe its own AI. The steering (fateN): the one drawn to win takes half while behind and three quarters while just ahead, and never goes under a tenth of their health while the other stands; the other takes a third more once the winner is under 30%. The bot plays to it too: drawn to lose and not behind, no shield, no team, a guard mostly down, slow to mash free, and the foe AI sharper (kAI + 0.5); drawn to win and 30% clear, she eases off (fewer blocks, slower taps). From a minute in, the loser takes more and more. So it can swing either way before it ends as drawn: tested, a villain win from 41 of 410 at 92 s, a hero win in 48 s. After 150 s it goes to the drawn winner ON POINTS. The prompts meant for a player (TAP, HOLD, DRAW, DODGE, KEEP GOING, HIT THEM NOW, NOT NOW...) are left off what is said; X IS DOWN instead of FINISHES YOU. Three seconds on the winner, then back to the TV cameras.
- Showdown: the villains (all three), the kaiju or the worm, with the heroes called at once. Odds: villains 42%, kaiju 55%, worm 60% for the heroes, give or take 10. Less to wear down than usual (each villain 420, the kaiju 560, the worm 480), so it settles in about a minute and a half (tested: the villains in 76 s, the kaiju in 81 s). The result is drawn and set on the old heroes' rolls (heroWin, heroes.js): a loss means one villain (picked at random) or the monster shrugs off the last blow. Settled when they fall back (heroLost) or when all are beaten / the monster's health runs out; no result in 160 s, or Next disaster, or Next town: the stake back.
- During a fight the bar is hidden (as in every fight) and Next disaster waits; Next town or the menu ends it and gives the stake back. The pause menu has no END THIS FIGHT in Watch.
- Test: watch-test.cjs (a Fight Night slip tapped with a stake of 250, fights drawn each way, a Showdown, What hits next with a stake of 50; coins and calls checked after each), watch-look.cjs (the slips at phone size).

## Punch hits: sound and spark options (Sean, 2026-11-05: hits sound like tin cans, the sparks look like cubes; the best lighting there can be; hits.html, hitsound.js, hitfx.js, hitspage.js)
- Options page, not yet in the fights: https://devon-cadaver.github.io/zday/hits.html. Nova throws jab, jab, cross at Tyrant on a street (night or day, slow motion, sound on or off), drawn through the game's own post (HDR, glow over white), so it looks as a fight would.
- Why today's sounds like a tin can: audio.js smack() adds two ringing partials (ping at 1900 and 3100 Hz). The new ones have none. Six to hear (hitsound.js, made like audio.js at 24 kHz; each made light (a jab) and heavy (a cross), three of each so a run is never the same): Today, Body blow (deep thump and a dull cloth slap), Movie punch (a crack on top, a big whump, a short tail), Heavy hitter (deeper, a crunch on the big ones), Snappy (short and tight for fast combos), Padded (a boxing glove's round thwack).
- Five spark looks (hitfx.js HitFx): Today (debris cubes and a flat star), Hot sparks (thin streaks drawn back along their speed, a few pixels wide at any distance, white hot cooling through yellow and orange to red, falling and skipping off the street), Sparks and light (those, a glowing core, and a real point light flashing from the blow onto both fighters, the wall and the street: two lights kept in the scene at zero so a flash never rebuilds the shaders), Energy hit (a flare, a sideways lens streak, a ring of light, sparks and light in the hero's colour), Impact, no sparks (a white flash, a shock ring and dust off the clothes).
- Once Sean picks: the sound replaces smack in audio.js (heavy blows the heavy one), and HitFx replaces the cubes in fighttest.js spark() and the crowd fight's spark().
- In the game (Sean picked Snappy and the Energy hit, coloured by whoever throws the blow, from their power): audio.js smack is Snappy (three made: light, middling, heavy). Every blow's spark is the energy hit (hitfx.js hitFxOf, one HitFx on the town's scene): the street fight's spark() (on the foe: the fighter's colour; on the fighter: the foe's; a block: the shield's blue; finishers and powers go through it too) and the crowd fight's spark() and sweep() (the blow that dusts one, in place of four grey cubes). Colours (tintOf): the knockback's own colour if it has one, else the stun's (POWER_COL; a kick, a car, a boulder have none): Nova red-pink (laser), Dragonfly pale cyan (gust), Tempo green (Guardian Shield), Tyrant purple (gravity), Blur gold (flash), Weaver blue (water); the villains behind the disasters by what they throw (FOE_COL), robots red. Past the third blow in a frame (a sweep through a crowd), small ones; sprites are reused, not made each time.
- The light: one PointLight added to the town's scene at load (main.js, scene.userData.hitLight), dark till a blow, so no shader is rebuilt when it first lights. Not on a phone (main.js low: a light costs every pixel there; ?q=high turns it on): there a flat pool of the blow's colour on the street stands in for it.

## Watch: the disasters as they are in Defend (Sean, 2026-11-05: the new versions, the heroes fighting them and able to lose, the villain after; bets on what, on the heroes, on the damage; tv.js, watchlevel.js)
- Superseded the same day (next section): Sean wants close shots only for playing; on TV the original look and big fights. watchlevel.js went for a day (back on 2026-11-06, below); its hooks stayed dormant (horde.js o.bot, o.watchK, o.bossK and the say filter; fighttest.js g.watchLevel), as do fightbot.js and the street fight's o.watch, which Watch no longer uses (no Fight Night either).
- Each town the broadcast lands in gets one disaster. WHAT HITS <TOWN>? (three kinds at their odds, drawn at them), then one slip with two rows: <hero> wins or <the disaster> wins, and Under or Over a line of buildings down, with the stake row (ALL IN on two bets puts half on each). The level plays out on the level's own camera (HERO CAM), both bets settle, a card (SAVED BY <HERO> or <THE DISASTER> WINS, n BUILDINGS DOWN: OVER/UNDER), and on to the next town; every third town a Fight Night first.
- The level is Defend's own (WatchLevel extends HeroGame, run inside the TV: its levels sized for television, its update, the villain fights; none of its XP or saves). The engines' questions to the mode (squad, side, round) go to the level through getters on the TV. One of your heroes plays it (picked at random from the hero side); one life: down once and the heroes have lost (the team still comes in on TEAM). The pick is put back after.
- On foot: aliens (100 soldiers, 2 tripods, 3 saucers), zombies (140), the worm and the kaiju (the giant fight), the riot (the mob and its three instigators), the earthquake (the Faultline). The villain after: the zombie or alien general flies in when the crowd is down to 25 (or at 100 s); THE BURROWER after the worm, THE BEAST KEEPER after the kaiju; the instigators after the mob. The Faultline has none in story mode, so none here.
- In the air (the weather levels, livestorm.js, set up as HeroGame.start and wallLevel do): the tornado (TWISTER after), the flood (CAPTAIN DELUGE with the hose, then by hand), the tsunami (RIPTIDE), the dust storm (THE DUST DEVIL), the cold wave (JACK FROST). Drawn to win, a front moves at 0.55 of its speed, so it can be beaten before it crosses the town (crossing it is a loss, as in Defend). The flying bot (airBot): PUNCH what is thrown as it comes within 22 m (GRAB it now and then within 15), 72% of the time drawn to win, 60% then 30% drawn to lose; the laser whenever it is full; TAP TAP TAP when it rears up or the beams meet (quick drawn to win, slow drawn to lose); DRAIN when the water is over 0.55; nothing coming, LOCK ON (the face, the man with the hose, the villain waiting). The storm villain charges into the fight on its own.
- The air's steering (airFate, no hooks in livestorm.js: what each frame took is added to or put back): drawn to win, what the storm loses is added to from 50 s on, she is kept over 15% health, and past 150 s the team sweeps in (the storm loses 4 a second, the laser filled); drawn to lose to it, she is kept over 25% for the first 55 s, then what she takes grows, and the storm cannot go under 35 (a blow can take 30). A street fight still going after 130 s (fightbot.js) is ended the way it was drawn.
- Nobody at the controls: a bot presses what a player would (watchlevel.js bot, called from horde.js frame through o.bot): the nearest in reach tapped (the lunge does the rest), walking in to the next, dashing off a red mark, through a quake ring (a dash passes under it), off a boulder's landing ring and a small giant's pound; GRAB on a landed boulder and let go (it homes in on the giant); the boss, a tripod's foot or a small giant walked up to and punched; TEAM when full, the knockback power now and then. Walled off from the crowd for 5 s: up (TAKE OFF) and locked on the nearest, down by it. Up in the air after a fight there: at the next general, or down by the nearest. Its street fights are fightbot.js (FightTest.start takes o.watch from the level).
- The result is drawn at the slip's odds (the kind's base: aliens 62%, zombies 66, worm 56, kaiju 50, riot 70, quake 52; 2.5 a level of the hero over the danger; give or take 8 for the night's form) and steered to: drawn to win, or to lose to the villain after (lossAt villain, 45% of the losses where there is one), she cannot be put down by the disaster (blows on her x0.6, health kept over 15), a giant takes x1.8 from her and more after 90 s, and past 150 s the team sweeps in (TEAM full, four of the crowd dusted a second, a saucer shot down, a giant hit); drawn to lose to the disaster, its blows grow (x1.1, more each second after 30 s) and a giant cannot go under 12 before she falls. The villain fights take the same draw (fightWatch: 'v' for the one she is to lose).
- The damage line: buildings down during the level (disasters.stats), over or under the median of the last nine of that kind on this device (zday_tvcalls.lines) plus a half; a first guess until there are three (aliens 30, zombies 8, worm 40, kaiju 35, riot 10, quake 18). Tested: zombies 13, kaiju 54 to 55, aliens 49, riot 13, worm 61, quake 5 to 18, tornado 7, flood 0, tsunami 17, dust storm 5, cold wave 2 (the air's first guesses: tornado 9, flood 1, tsunami 12, dust storm 6, cold wave 5).
- In a level: daylight (phase 0.3, no zombie night: too dark to follow), the controls, the squad icons, the TEAM buttons and the flight bar hidden; the bet strip shows the bets and BUILDINGS DOWN n · LINE x; the prompts meant for a player left off what is said. Next disaster waits for the level; Next town or the menu ends it and gives the stakes back.
- Tested (lv-test.cjs, flow-test.cjs; a built copy served on its own port, since the shared dev server reloads on the other session's edits): zombies won with the general beaten (105 s), the kaiju lost to THE BEAST KEEPER (255 s, since sped up), aliens lost to the invasion after the general was beaten (110 s), the riot won through three instigator fights (105 s), the worm won with THE BURROWER beaten (115 s), the earthquake won (125 to 150 s); the whole flow with real taps on both slips, paid right, on to the next town; then the tornado won through TWISTER (55 s), the tsunami lost (75 s), the flood won through CAPTAIN DELUGE (62 s), the dust storm lost to THE DUST DEVIL, the cold wave won through JACK FROST (62 s). No page errors.

## The walk and the guard (Sean, 2026-11-06: the walk a little stiff, the arms always up in a fight pose; hands up only near an enemy; fighttest.js Body.pose, atEase)
- Each body is told how near its nearest foe is (body.near, metres): the street fight both fighters, the distance between them, every frame; the crowd fight the hero, the nearest zombie, general, boss or Faultline giant, five times a second (horde.js); the base Infinity (nobody there to fight). Unset (the Pose Lab, anything else) it keeps the guard, as before.
- Standing or walking in the guard clip: under 4.5 m the guard, over 7.5 m at ease, between them a blend (calm, eased: up in about a tenth of a second, down over half a second). At ease (atEase): the arms hanging a little bent at the sides, the body standing easy; standing, a slow breath and a look about.
- The walk: the legs swing as before, the swinging knee folds more and the standing one is never locked, the heel down and the toe off, the hips turning one way and the shoulders the other with the head held to the front, the weight going from foot to foot, a little lean. At ease the arms swing against the legs, the elbow bending as each comes forward; in the guard they stay up and bob. The body's real speed (body.spd, from how far it moved) eases it into a run from 3.5 to 6.5 m/s: elbows bent to about a right angle and pumping, more lean, the head up (walkK goes to full whenever it moves, so it could not tell a walk from a run).

## A looseness pass over all moves (HERO-PLAN, Sean, 2026-11-06; fighttest.js Body.springs, LOOSE)
- One layer over every move instead of each clip by hand: the torso, chest, head, shoulders, elbows and wrists follow the pose through damped springs (stiffness and damping per joint, LOOSE), a few small steps a frame. The hips, legs and feet stay exact so the feet stay planted, and pins and IK (a hand on a neck) still land exactly, as they come after.
- What it does: the snap of a move leads and the rest follows; a punch's arm overshoots a little and settles, the head whips further on a blow and rocks back, the chest keeps turning after the hips. Measured against the plain pose: up to about 30 degrees behind at the snap of a cross or an uppercut (the cross's elbow 32 degrees behind at 50 ms, nearly caught up by the blow at 100 ms), back on the pose within about half a second.
- Only on bodies in play (body.loose: the street fight's two, the crowd fight's hero, the base). The Pose Lab's bodies are left plain, so a pose looks there exactly as it was made; a whirl that drives the arms itself (armsA) keeps them exact.

## Outfits that open with levels (HERO-PLAN item 6's not yet; Sean, 2026-11-06; unlocks.js, wardrobe.js)
- The plain pieces from the start; the showier ones open as each fighter levels up (their own level): face War paint 2, Full mask 3, Skull paint 4, Android 6, Robot 8; back Torn cape 2, Jet pack 3, Feather wings 4, Bat wings 5, Insect wings 6; Shoulder plates 2, Spiked shoulders 4; Harness 2, Breastplate 3; Spiked forearms 3; headgear from 2 (goggles, visor, ninja mask, fedora, cowboy hat) to 8 (the pumpkins), the lair's own from 2 (demon horns) to 8 (spike halo). Colours, hair, body and skin are always free.
- In the wardrobe a piece not open yet is dimmed with a lock and the level (a tap says when it opens); the arrows on the headgear go round only what is open. What a fighter already wears stays on, whatever its level (no save loses anything).
- A level up's line after a level lists what just opened: New in the wardrobe: ... (a hero is not told of the lair's headgear).

## More team moves (HERO-PLAN 10e's not yet; Sean, 2026-11-06; fighttest.js and horde.js teamGo, mateOwn)
- TEAM now calls a teammate's own move, from their knockback power, in turn with the classic (each teammate counts their own calls, from the start of each fight): a shield-bearer (Guardian Shield, Tempo's) always shields you.
- Street fight: a power that shoots (laser, gust, water cannon, fire jet, lightning, air cannon, ice ram, repulsor, whirlwind, tidal slam, sonic boom, solar flare, black hole, thunderclap: RANGED_TEAM) is a BLAST: the teammate hovers 5 m up off the foe and fires their power's beam in its colour; the foe blown away (30). Any other is a LAUNCH: in low, the foe knocked high (18) for you to chase and spike (UP! CHASE THEM!). The classic is the blow and their stun (25). So Nova blasts with her laser, Dragonfly with the gust, Weaver with water; Tyrant and Blur launch; Tempo shields.
- Crowd fight: the BLAST is their beam swept from her out 35 m ahead through the crowd; the other is a SLAM down into the nearest pack ahead, everything within 7 m knocked flat; the classic is the pass through the crowd with their stun.

## Watch: the battle for the town (Sean, 2026-11-05: "the fights in Disaster TV should be the pulled back heli shots or rooftop shots (only sometimes). The close up shots should only be used for actual game play. Disaster TV should plan on the original look of the game, with the forced perspective and dramatic big fights between the heroes, villains, disasters, and rescue / army"; tv.js, tvcam.js)
- Superseded on 2026-11-06 (next section): Sean meant Defend's own fights, filmed from the helicopters; the town battle (the old disasters, two villains, the old heroes) gave a plain tornado and random villains, and its zombie spill turned the night on and off.
- The town's own look all the time: the tilt-shift miniature filmed by the news helicopters (SKY 7, SKY 4), pulled back (at least 300 m, 1.4 the follow distance; was 220, 1.2), a viewer's rooftop video only sometimes (a fifth of the cuts from a helicopter; was two fifths). Both helicopters lost to a villain or the kaiju: the station sends up another (SKY n) rather than living on rooftop videos. No fight camera, no level camera, no Fight Night.
- Each town: WHAT HITS <TOWN>? (three of aliens, zombies, worm, kaiju, riot, earthquake, tornado, flood, tsunami, dust storm, cold wave at their odds, drawn at them), then the battle slip: <THE DISASTER> AND <TWO VILLAINS>, two rows (the heroes win or the villains win; under or over a line of buildings down) and the stake row. Then the battle, the result, and on to the next town 8 s after.
- The battle: the town's own disaster (disasters.js; zombies are the chemical spill that starts an outbreak; the kaiju, worm, aliens and riot stay 150 s, the tornado 120), two of the villains (villains.js: the villain side's pick and one of the others, 600 health each, with their own AI, throwing cars at the cameras as before), the three heroes on the hero signal 6 s in (heroes.js: Nova, Dragonfly, Tempo with their old jobs: each villain to its hero, the others to the kaiju, the worm, the aliens, people to ferry), the rescue services and the army rolling the whole time (response.js respond: fire trucks and water drops, rescue helicopters, police and armoured squads, storm chasers and cloud seeding, airstrikes on the kaiju and the alien machines, rescue boats).
- The heroes' bet is drawn at the slip's odds (the kind's base, 50 to 66%, give or take 10) and set on the heroes' own rolls (heroWin): drawn to win, every villain's last blow lands; drawn to lose, one of them (picked at random) shrugs it off and the heroes fall back (villains.js beat). The kaiju and the worm too: driven off, or (half the time in a loss) the heroes swatted away. Settled when every villain is beaten (THE HEROES WIN) or one holds (<VILLAIN> WINS, THE HEROES FALL BACK); not settled in 150 s, the draw is carried out (the rest beaten, or the one drawn to hold says so).
- The damage: buildings down from the start of the battle until 25 s after the heroes' bet is settled, or the disaster is over; over or under the median of the last nine of that kind on this device plus a half (zday_tvcalls.lines2; the story levels' lines are left alone), a first guess till there are three (aliens 40, zombies 10, worm 30, kaiju 35, riot 10, quake 25, tornado 40, flood 10, tsunami 30, dust storm 15, cold wave 12). Tested: kaiju 37 and 77, tornado 69, aliens 69, zombies 26.
- The director: the helicopter pans (no cut) between the disaster and one of the fights every 12 to 16 s, and to the fight that settled it. The heroes' and villains' news on the ticker (herolost, villainbeaten).
- From Sean's iPhone (2026-11-05, on the story-level version): the announcer's words (radio.js #zd-radio) filled the screen from the top bar to the ticker: body.zd-flying put its top at 66 px and Watch's rule its bottom over the ticker. Watch's rule now also sets top:auto and wins (html body.zd-tv), so it stays a strip over the ticker whatever else is on. The minimap and the compass are hidden on Watch (a broadcast, not a game). His other notes (the hero standing still against the dust storm in third person, no helicopter shots, the street fight's ring) were about the level version and went with it.
- Tested (bt-test.cjs on a built copy, real taps on the slip): a kaiju with Blur won (56 s), a tornado with Weaver lost (125 s), aliens with Weaver won (115 s), zombies with Tyrant lost, a kaiju with Tyrant and Weaver lost (Tyrant beaten, Weaver held, 150 s). No page errors.

## The tornado fought on foot (HERO-PLAN 10d's not yet; Sean, 2026-11-06; twisterfoot.js, herogame.js twisterLevel)
- A tornado on the story's board is played on foot every other time (footTornado: the first on foot, the next in the air with its storm game, and so on), in the crowd fight in the Faultline's place (horde o.twister: the same hooks).
- The town's tornado (twister.js, in its steered mode) circles her about 24 m off, closing in and backing off, tearing up the streets. Within about 16 m it pulls her in (IT IS PULLING YOU IN: DASH OUT!); right on it, it throws her out (a bite). Every 3 to 5 s it flings a car from 18 m up at where she is going, a red ring where it lands; under it, a bite.
- A landed car glows (four at most, 18 s each). GRAB by it (or by where a drawn stroke's dash began) carries it (A CAR! LET GO TO THROW IT INTO THE FUNNEL); let go and it flies into the funnel (INTO THE FUNNEL! n/5). Each hit shrinks it; five (more with danger) and it ropes out (IT'S BREAKING UP!), and Twister comes out of it 2.5 s later (horde ctrlMake) for the street fight, as the worm's and the kaiju's drivers. Beaten, the town is saved. Punching the funnel only says YOU CANNOT PUNCH WIND.
- The camera frames the funnel and her together (focus), as for the Faultline.
- Test: twister-test.cjs (from the board, a car flung and landed, the pull 10 to 8 m in 0.7 s, a car grabbed with a stroke and thrown with a swipe for 1/5, the rest, Twister's fight, TOWN SAVED with the wardrobe's new pieces in the level up's line).
- Fixed (2026-11-06): in its steered mode the tornado sized itself from what it ate (Be The Disaster's way), so a hit's shrink did not hold and the funnel was not remade; now it does not grow by eating in this level and each hit remakes the funnel smaller (1.9 to about 1.0 over the five).

## The story's mix and its beats (HERO-PLAN: tune the mix; story beats that hung on the old level order need new triggers; Sean, 2026-11-06)
- The board (threats.js): the two giant cities are one worm and one kaiju (they were two worms; the kaiju came only now and then); a board from before keeps its worm cities as the giants. The rotating city is a level of its own (zombies, the riot, the earthquake) three times in four, else one of the older disasters with a villain (killer plant, volcano, rift, sinkhole, black hole, toxic spill). With no villains left to fight, the villain city is one of the levels of their own (was the kaiju). Tested: 40 rotating draws gave 29 of the three, 11 older; 20 giant wins brought 11 worms and 9 kaiju.
- Who you meet when, on the new trigger of whom a fighter has actually met: the first time each of your fighters fights someone in the street (the three villains, the heroes as rivals, the alien and zombie generals, Twister, Riptide, the Dust Devil, Jack Frost, the Burrower, the Beast Keeper, the riot's three), a card: FIRST MEETING, the name (your own name for one of the six), and a line. Kept with the fighter (chars[id].met), so each meets them in their own time; not in Watch or the practice ring.
- The pick screen's cards said Next: and a level from the old fixed order; they say Towns saved: now.

## The main menu's layout (Sean, 2026-11-06, from his iPhone: a hero half in the globe; the whole globe on a phone; the title small on one line with the XP bar at the top, smaller; the saucer lower, the globe centred)
- game.js showMenu: MY LITTLE DISASTER on one line at 24 px (the PLAY screen keeps its big two-line logo), the tagline under it; on the main panel the Legend and Levels won moved up into the title block, the Legend in two rows (Hero XP, LEGEND and its name, Villain XP; then the bar), 262 px wide so it clears the sound buttons on the right. The saucer alone in the box at the bottom; held upright on a short phone it is a little smaller (max-width min(440px, 110dvh - 330px)) so the globe keeps its room.
- globe.js menuFit: the whole globe (a 12% margin) in the gap between the title block and the top of the saucer's dome (the box top plus 11% of its height); on a phone on its side the whole screen as before. 375x636: the globe 228 px across, centred at 276.
- watchers: the three heroes placed by where the globe's edge is on the screen (their feet at 1.24 to 1.34 globe radii out, kept 30 px inside the screen's sides), worked out from the menu's camera distance, so they are never in the globe however it is fitted, and a pinch in still sends them off the screen. A little bigger (3 from 2.5). The secret button's knock flies them off as before.

## Watch: Defend's fights, filmed from the helicopters (Sean, 2026-11-06: "It should be just like the Defend game play but from a helicopter... after the tornado monster is beaten, the hero fights the villain that was controlling the tornado. This should be like this for all disaster fights"; tv.js, tvcam.js, watchlevel.js)
- Each town: WHAT HITS <TOWN>? (three of the eleven at their odds), then <THE DISASTER> vs <HERO> (one of your heroes, the villain after named; heroes win or lose, under or over a line of buildings down), then the level exactly as in Defend (watchlevel.js, the computer playing, steered to the draw, as on 2026-11-05 above), then the result card and the next town. No Fight Night.
- Filmed by the TV only: tvcam.js runs through the level; each engine's frame (horde, flight, the street fight) is wrapped while a level runs (wrapPilots) and after it the TV's view is put back (afterPilot): the helicopter's position and aim, its fov, the town's depth of field (the tilt-shift miniature), the near plane, focus and detail for a view from further off, no camera blends. The street fight's air ring is hidden, and so is the dust storm's half-blind haze.
- What the helicopters frame (tv.js watchPoint, read by tvcam.want): the hero and what she fights, both in shot: a street fight from 75 m; on foot 95 m; the kaiju 240 m, the worm 190; the Faultline 150 + 4.5 m a metre of its height; in the air the storm's face (or the funnel) and her, 140 m or 1.6 times the gap. Aimed at their height (a fight in the air is up off the street). Rooftop videos a fifth of the cuts; the kaiju and villains do not go for the cameras during a level.
- In the air the hero circles the storm (45 to 60 m out, closing in and pulling back) between her punches, grabs, lasers and charges, so she is not a still dot; early in a fight drawn to win she sends back half of what is thrown (72% after 45 s), so a storm lasts longer.
- None of the game's own screen on TV: the fight and level HUDs, the flight bar and HUD, the storms' bars and buttons, the TAP TAP TAP overlay, the squad icons and the game's minimap and compass are hidden; the TV's own buttons stay. The bet strip carries a scoreboard: <HERO> 84% vs THE KAIJU 45% (or 63 LEFT, THE FAULTLINE, TWISTER 53%), then BUILDINGS DOWN n · LINE x.
- Daylight in a level (phase 0.3) and no zombie night, as in Defend, so the night does not come and go with the zombies.
- Tested (lv-test.cjs on a built copy): the tornado with its face, then TWISTER in the air (helicopter shots and a viewer's video of the face); the dust storm then THE DUST DEVIL; the kaiju (from 240 m, over the town) then THE BEAST KEEPER, lost as drawn; zombies then the zombie general, daylight all through; the Faultline over the roofs. No page errors.

## The TV scripts page (Sean, 2026-11-06: the bullet points for each disaster, a notes box for each, a picture for each point; public/tv-scripts.html)
- devon-cadaver.github.io/zday/tv-scripts.html, a plain page in public/ (no build step): how each town goes, then the eleven disasters, each point beside a picture from Watch (public/tv-scripts/<kind>-<n>.jpg, 30 of them), tap for full size; a notes box under each and one for the whole show, kept on the device as typed (zday_tvscript_notes), and Copy all my notes (the clipboard, or the text shown to copy by hand) to paste back to Claude.
- The pictures (scratchpad cap2.cjs, on a built copy): each disaster started from Watch as a drawn win, later beats brought on (the crowd thinned but for 22 so the general still comes, the giant's heroHp set to 0, the storm's win(), the Faultline's hits, the swell and the beam duel started), and for each picture the camera handed back to the level's own (wrapPilots(false)) with the blur off, so the enemy is clear; the broadcast's captions hidden. On TV the same moments are filmed from the helicopters.
